## 29 October 2026 | Rigorous Founder-friction cross-workflow audit (PR #415 draft)

**Founder invariant:** one Founder, one phone, minimal-to-zero routine intervention. Distinguish self-service account registration, evidence reading, verified qualification, live paid-duty eligibility and payout from each other. Never require a Founder approval click for clean source-confirmed outcomes. Never declare an OCR-readable policy active without authenticated carrier/broker confirmation or clear a paid Operator without valid statutory screening. Current Florida statutory source checked: 2026 Fla. Stat. §627.748.

**Verified implementation review (source, NOT full live commissioning):**
1. **Documents:** current public build forces manual review even for clean OCR and original PDF. In this draft, clean photo / PDF / HEIC / HEIF / WebP / PNG / JPEG within 5 MiB can receive `accept` from deterministic reading and feed authoritative qualification; ambiguous documents remain `review`. OCR runs locally and is bounded (one serialized Tesseract worker per container, MAX_QUEUE=8, 45-second per-job deadline), so million-Operator throughput and real-format accuracy are NOT certified. Synthetic format tests are not real-device or real-carrier benchmarks.
2. **Operator checklist:** `app/operator/qualify.tsx` used to demand an unnecessary final "Submit for Review" tap even when complete. Draft now auto-submits the already-complete checklist once per state transition, with the server independently reassessing all statutory evidence. No Founder button is needed for fully authenticated cases.
3. **Insurance continuing-status:** existing provider-neutral confirmation emails, short-lived confirmation links, 60-day confirmation clock and verified official agency contact lookup support automatic confirmation when an independent source responds. BUT fresh provider contact identity can require named Ops trust; OCR of declarations page does not attest current insurer status; the Operator currently still initiates the confirmation step. This is a **real unresolved instant-onboarding bottleneck** for unfamiliar insurers. Reuploading a policy currently resets monitoring to unverified; investigate policy-identity-preserving idempotence rather than restarting status when the unchanged verified policy is re-sent.
4. **Screening:** existing provider-neutral workflow `/operator/screening/existing`, `screening-handoff.js` and `external-screening.js` require named Ops to authenticate CRA reports. A Checkr provider adapter exists in source but is not wired as an active zero-touch default in the current external-screening flow. No real verified report may be fabricated from an Operator's self-declaration. **High-priority unfinished automation.** A trusted authenticated CRA API/webhook can set clear/refusal and preserve adverse-action rights without a routine human reviewer.
5. **Recruiter:** a Founder-issued invitation already self-approves on acceptance; referral links currently require agreement, identity and Stripe payout eligibility even before first earned commission. That couples acquisition to payout setup and is additional friction; consider decoupling link/accrual from payout readiness while preserving identity, agreement, caps, fraud controls, and no transfers without connected Stripe. Recruiter readiness is not a prerequisite for paid Traveler booking.
6. **Geographic growth:** Operator document and screening intake are guarded by active operating market; waitlist is available elsewhere. Consider allowing pre-enrollment where no paid dispatch is authorized, without implying counties are commercially open.
7. **Travel payment / dispatch:** on-demand supply precheck before Stripe authorization, verified payment before assignment; scheduled requires an off-session method. Preserve this deliberate no-hold-when-empty design. No global recruiter, screening workflow config, or artificial initial fleet condition should block individually lawful paid operations.
8. **Founder access:** authenticated private Ops interface supports document/recruiter/market action; the installed custom Founder Commands plugin's authorized tool surface is NOT available in this chat, and ChatGPT Work browser requires its own authenticated session. Do not claim direct production approval or visibility into private document details from the current chat.
9. **Scale:** a synchronous single-worker 45s local OCR pool with MAX_QUEUE=8 is not proven sufficient for millions of Operators; benchmark and isolate elastic document workers/jobs from payment/dispatch capacity before global growth.

**Concrete action in this draft:** document format OCR + deterministic clear-document decisions, truthful persisted review status, zero-click final submit, compact native SSO and associated tests. **Not yet implemented:** live default CRA integration, zero-touch independent insurer source onboarding for every carrier, elastic high-volume OCR benchmark, Recruiter referral/payout separation, premarket document enrollment. These are material items and must not be described as completed.

## 29 October 2026 | Founder standard enforced: automated Operator document intake (draft PR #415, NOT LIVE)

**Non-negotiable operating model:** one Founder, one device, minimal human intervention. Manual approval of every Operator document was an implementation defect, not a founder requirement. Qualified Operators should commission automatically when all independently authenticated eligibility facts pass; hold only genuine mismatches/unreadable documents/external checks for exception handling. No global human-approval queue as a routine gate.

**Implementation in draft:** `backend/documents.js` reader version 4 no longer overwrites every deterministic `accept` with `review`. `backend/localocr.js` extracts name, licence number, expiry, registration plate/VIN and commercial coverage fields from clear evidence and applies explicit confidence/field-match rules. `backend/document-convert.js` is a new bounded, shell-free, private local processor for native and scanned PDFs (Poppler first two pages), JPEG and PNG (Tesseract), WebP (dwebp conversion), HEIC and HEIF (libheif conversion). These open-source native helpers are installed in `backend/Dockerfile`. No metered per-document AI, no external OCR endpoint and no individual Founder clicks for clean documents. Safe 5 MiB cap remains for every accepted type; the original declarations page is sufficient when it contains the legally required coverage facts, not the entire policy.

**Eligibility safeguard:** An OCR-readable policy is not proof of live coverage. `backend/qualification.js` still enforces statutory coverage, matching insured and vehicle, current authenticated carrier/broker confirmation, provider-verified screening and duty/payout eligibility. Existing independent insurer confirmation by authenticated broker/carrier requests can progress automatically. Unclear and low-confidence extraction is `review`, not automatic approval or ungrounded refusal. Mobile qualification/docs/insurance keep the persisted `Under Review` state. Server and UI test assertions cover clean OCR, wrong-person, unavailable reader, unsupported file, PDF-native and scanned flows, and exact Google/Apple native controls.

**Release truth:** This work is on a draft feature branch, not merged or deployed, and not in Build 57. No production Operator has been newly approved by this source change. Do not claim scalable million-Operator throughput or live document benchmarks from unit tests; do not activate users until test suite and authentic insurer/screening integrations prove the intended flow. Source tests plus signed iPhone/Android device checks are required. The 36pt official native Apple/15pt Google visual fix remains part of the same draft PR.

## 29 October 2026 | Final founder revision: retain 5 MiB evidence, compact native Apple SSO, truthful review status (PR #415 draft)

**Supersedes earlier 15 MiB and enlarged-Google proposals in the same PR.** Founder prefers a 5 MiB upload cap matching current safe local OCR memory bound; original evidence must be up to 5 MiB per document on mobile and in the authenticated Operations viewer, with all five localized Operator upload hints reverted to 5 MB. PDFs and unreadable/unsupported images under 5 MiB still need named review. The present local OCR reads eligible JPEG/PNG/WebP images but intentionally never automatically approves any document, because authenticity and current commercial insurance cannot be inferred from text alone. Operator manual-review exception appears in `/ops` qualification queue, with private original viewing. Existing mobile correction that persists `review` (rather than `todo`) and shows **Under Review** across qualification, documents and insurance remains; **no reupload requirement** merely for a valid pending file. Secure availability and screening checks remain mandatory.

**Small-sign-in preference and guidelines:** Apple HIG permits a 30pt minimum Sign in with Apple button and recommends 44pt; official native Apple text/logo are controlled by its height, not a custom font size. Reduce official native Apple visible control to 36pt, retaining a full 46pt accessible parent touch region; preserve the founder-approved Google 15pt title and 17pt unmodified SDK G, display both at 36pt visual height with expanded touch region via hitSlop. Apple system control remains genuine and localizable. Confirm final optical balance and touch targets on a signed iOS build (and Google appearance on Android), not merely through CI. These are UNMERGED source modifications and were not added to Build 57.

## 29 October 2026 | Founder-identified OCR-size intake gap closed in draft PR #415 (not live)

**Observed source defect:** Original Operator evidence 5–15 MiB uploads privately and can be inspected in authenticated Operations, but the 5 MiB safe local OCR limit returns `verdict:'review'`. `backend/qualification.js` properly classifies that as an **exception pending source verification**, never `document_missing` or `document_refused`. However, the iPhone state store previously mapped both `review` and `refuse` to `todo`, and the qualification checklist showed **Add**, falsely suggesting a received document was missing. This is misleading and risks repeat upload churn.

**Draft correction:** Preserve an explicit persisted document state `review`; the qualification checklist shows **Under Review** and the documents/insurance pages also show human review after app restart. Only a server `accept` is `ok` and counts toward verified; denied/missing docs remain unverified. Safe OCR limit stays 5 MiB to bound reading cost/CPU; a larger file's private original is viewable up to 15 MiB and now returns nonalarmist wording `Document received. Automatic text reading was skipped ...`. The strict insurance/screening/continuing-status gates and individual audit trail are unchanged. New qualification regression checks both the server classification and persisted UI distinction. These are **draft mobile/backend changes**, not deployed or in TestFlight Build 57.

**SSO visual correction in same PR:** 46pt Apple official native button untouched; custom Google button remains 46pt, increases Google title from 15pt to 19pt and preserves unmodified official Google G at 20pt rather than 17pt. Source-contract tests can confirm sizes and asset identity, NOT real signed-device visual equality. User-facing 'will this be correct?' answer is: **plausibly improved, must inspect next signed iPhone and Android builds**. Do not claim Apple native font size can be controlled.

## 29 October 2026 | Founder-approved 15 MiB private evidence allowance, search/payment review and Apple recording

**Scope in draft PR #415, not merged/deployed:** Founder directed one bundled next iOS/Android release rather than a separate upload-limit build. Increase Operator photo/PDF private evidence uploads from 5 MiB to **15 MiB per file** in `src/backend/documentUpload.ts` and increase authenticated named-review byte limit to 15 MiB in `backend/operator-document-review.js`. Add assertions that large (6 MiB) PDFs remain inspectable by named Operations and >15 MiB evidence is refused. The existing bounded **5 MiB local OCR** remains fail-safe: oversized evidence requests manual review rather than automatically approving or rejecting insurance. A complete multi-page insurance policy remains **not mandatory**; the app requests the declarations page.

**Real on-demand search:** `backend/geooperators.js` queries geohash precisions 5 then 4, nine bounded prefixes per tier, 40 rows then up to 120 per saturated prefix. This is not a circular radius search. Around central Miami (25.76/-80.19), the 9-cell outer tier has a theoretical geographic corner ~46 straight-line miles away; at other positions and latitudes the exact envelope varies. The current `/travel/prepare` checks live eligible fleet immediately **before payment** and denies `no_operator` if empty. If supply exists, Stripe's sheet authorizes the single Travel. `/travel/dispatch` independently requires Stripe-confirmed payment or hold and a still-eligible Operator. There is no fixed repeating 60-second search; manual `Try Again` exists. Council-inspired future recommendation, NOT implemented: assess short 15-second offer windows / bounded 30–60-second reoffer, ETA rather than pure crow-flight distance as fleet grows. Do not substitute the research recommendation for a live configuration.

**Review evidence and account deletion:** The existing 6m13s physical-device Build 57 recording demonstrates the in-app Account Termination Protocol. The founder confirms that after pressing the final **Yes, Delete** control the app returns to its sign-in screen; source invokes authenticated account deletion before clearing the signed-in session. This is user-observed deletion flow evidence, though without an independent read of the Firebase Auth/Firestore records it is not a separate server-data deletion audit. No account-deletion email notification is automatically sent by the current implementation, and connected Gmail had verification emails only. Preserve separate reviewer demo credentials; do NOT delete the App Review account. An empty production fleet explains why an on-device paid pickup/arrival/completion cannot be recorded honestly; explain that clearly to App Review and provide truthful demo account access and supplemental synthetic testing, not a fake successful ride.

**Strong independent evidence the MAESTRO approach itself is failing:** Minimal [QA dual-driver probe 40902331541](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/actions/runs/40902331541) on a relatively clean macOS runner using Apple's **Settings app only**, without American Rider app/backend/payment fixtures, ended **FAILURE**. Both separate Maestro invocations returned `IOSDriverTimeoutException` during XCTest runner startup, even with distinct host ports; evidence artifact `qa-ios-xctest-pair-probe` ID **12910926448**. This proves the instrumented two-simulator approach can fail without American Rider involvement and is consistent with repeated XCTest driver infrastructure failures. It does NOT prove all real-device app navigation works, nor does it attribute all prior failures to identical causes. **Stop requiring this brittle cloud two-Maestro approach as the sole acceptance/publication prerequisite.**

**STRICT UNVERIFIED BOUNDARY:** These 94 checks run offline/in a synthetic environment, not signed iOS/Android device UI and not actual provider execution. The available connected Stripe session exposes **one live-mode account**, not a TEST-mode Stripe account; **NO Stripe API writes/charges were sent**. Source and deterministic TEST-policy cases passing does NOT establish an actual `sk_test_` PaymentIntent authorization/capture, Connect transfer, refund, real bank arrival or production webhook. Existing protected manual GitHub workflow `.github/workflows/stripe-manual-sandbox-acceptance.yml` can run **TEST-only** with a correctly paired secret; its known coverage does not include Connect transfers. Distinct same-Travel Stripe TEST capture + Connect transfer provider reconciliation remains REQUIRED for paid commercialization. Insurer/CRA true verification, commercial operator eligibility, new-user end-to-end signup, public Recruiter bank payments, web install handoff on signed devices, GPS and actual notification flows remain unverified by this matrix. Source flags for referrals/Recruiters must be checked against authenticated production settings before assuming they are enabled.

**Next cheapest truthful release strategy:** (1) accept the **six proven backend groups** without rerunning expensive Maestro for them; (2) run narrow actual TEST-mode Stripe authorization/capture/transfer/refund and reconcile provider IDs with the same local Travel using protected TEST credentials only (never live-mode); (3) test a REAL signed physical Traveler and Operator device pair and screenshot app recovery, login/OAuth, live UI navigation, physical GPS and one TEST-mode paid Travel, with no fake production Operator; (4) verify invitation/referral website-to-app transitions by native OS/universal link on real devices, separately from optional Recruiter payout; (5) once actual safety, marketplace and store-review criteria pass, proceed with review-ready Build 57 only with founder release authorization. Apple does NOT require a Maestro two-simulator run, but does expect tested on-device functional app and complete review account data; do not equate source PASS with App Store approval. **Build 57 and public paid operations remain HELD pending unverified external/physical gates.**

## 29 October 2026 | FOUNDER AUDIT: STOP REPEATED MAESTRO EXPERIMENTS; SEPARATE TESTING FROM APP STORE RELEASE

**Founder directive and documented correction:** The founder observes multiple days of repeated iOS automation errors and repeated recommendations. Treat this as a **testing-process failure requiring de-duplication and evidence-based stopping rules**. Before proposing another "isolate simulator", "prewarm CoreSimulator", "increase timeout", "separate driver port", "preserve screenshot", or "re-run same flow", check this entry, [incident #399](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/399), previous attempts and exact GitHub run logs. An old recommendation is NOT a new fix simply because the wording changes. The previous 29 October entry below was only an early snapshot; this entry records the now-final result and supersedes that snapshot.

**Actual repetitions already attempted:** iOS sign-in and operator duty passed repeatedly, including [40875456667](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/actions/runs/40875456667), [40884833413](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/actions/runs/40884833413), and [40892501177](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/actions/runs/40892501177). Repeated failures had **different** causes: Maestro clipboard and UI state mismatch [40879383424]; CoreSimulator enumerate/boot/location delays [40890018946], [40884833413]; dual XCTest driver **startup** timeout [40875456667]; and later dual drivers successfully started but **accessibility hierarchy / lifecycle transport** timed out [40892501177]. Earlier QA changes: isolated roles/drivers with distinct ports, timeout and evidence instrumentation, checksum-reused iOS binary, SDK-runtime matching, a clean-room simulator enumeration [40891282558] (passed 5.27 seconds), early two-device selection, and moving Traveler GPS into device-scoped Maestro. Those are **already tried**, not validated general solutions. Do not blame the app or payment system when no native user interaction reached it.

**Definitive 40892501177 result:** Step 34 concurrent Traveler/Operator journey **FAILED**, but first five of eight evidence checkpoints **PASSED**: isolated fixture, Operator eligibility, native installation, real Operator signed in/In Service, Traveler installed. Two Maestro/XCTest drivers served 7101/7102; Operator first viewHierarchy query timed out querying iOS status bar (~192 seconds), Traveler launchApp(clearState: true) hung during terminateApp. Native offer/booking, completion and same-Travel Stripe TEST reconciliation **NOT VERIFIED**. Evidence artifact `native-commercial-ios-evidence` ID **12909705920**, ~51.6 MB. QA-only draft [PR #403](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/pull/403) and [probe 40902331541](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/actions/runs/40902331541) test concurrent XCTest hierarchy transport in a clean macOS runner using Apple's Settings app; as of documentation, no result certified. This is an incremental host/driver hypothesis check, **not** a newly invented solution or proof of the American Rider app.

**Crucial test-vs-app distinction:** An infrastructure failure before app UI, operator offer, Stripe PaymentSheet or API execution gives **NO app failure verdict** on those unentered business stages. A reliable screenshot/network/provider trace showing an actual app error or failed server/Stripe reconciliation is required to identify a product defect. Treat statuses independently as PASS, APP FAIL, QA TOOL FAIL, ENVIRONMENT FAIL, or UNTESTED. Stop defaulting to a single end-to-end two-simulator Maestro suite as the sole publication gate. Green CI/static backend checks are not real-world booking evidence either.

**Publication requirements and alternative validation plan (Apple primary sources, checked Oct 29):** Apple's App Review Guidelines 2.1 and developer documentation require a working, non-crashing app tested on **physical compatible devices**, complete review notes, valid demo credentials and functioning backend; they **do not mandate Maestro or a two-iPhone simulator suite**. Apple documentation: https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/ and https://developer.apple.com/help/app-review/before-submitting-for-review/test-compatible-devices and https://developer.apple.com/documentation/xcode/testing and https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/test-a-beta-version/testflight-overview . Use a **test pyramid**: (1) inexpensive API/Firestore/Firebase/Stripe TEST contract, idempotency, dispatch and settlement checks, reconciling the SAME Travel; (2) single-device smoke and targeted UI automation without redundant full resets; (3) **signed TestFlight on physical iPhones**, and Android equivalent, with a manual witnessed paired Traveler/Operator end-to-end case and negative recovery cases; (4) request Apple review only when the app is stable and reviewer can access actual functionality with supplied role demo accounts, relevant regulatory documentation and no false commercial availability. Two simultaneously automated cloud simulators are **optional**, not a condition imposed by Apple. Avoid production charges during testing. No physical-device, insurer/CRA, live payout, or App Store acceptance is asserted merely because a test passes.

**Founder release decision boundary:** Separate **technical submission** from **public paid-operation authorization**. TestFlight/Store review can advance independently of custom Maestro infra failures where signed-device usability and review prerequisites are satisfied, but public paid bookings must not be proclaimed safe on the basis of five of eight checkpoints: verify actual paired dispatch + Stripe TEST capture and Operator transfer, real eligibility and production safety/legal gates. Build 57 remains held pending an intentional founder-approved review-ready build and evidence review; do not silently release/deploy. Any next automation experiment must have a **novel discriminating question**, cost/time cap, unique evidence and **stop rule**. If the clean paired driver probe passes but the fully loaded job fails, stop repeating it and move to realistic device/limited tests; if it fails, classify macOS/CoreSimulator transport as harness failure and use the alternative strategy. See the older Oct 27–29 records below; preserve history without claiming past incomplete snapshots are live statuses.

## 27 October 2026 | FOUNDER-DIRECTED NATIVE QA ECONOMY: BUILD ONCE, TEST MANY, PROVE THE JOURNEY

**Standing decision:** The Founder identified repeated, expensive Android/iOS native rebuilds after Maestro-only test-script fixes. Stop treating script failures as reasons to recompile an unchanged app. Keep one authoritative, auditable native QA campaign; do not start overlapping device jobs or cancel a compiling run merely to edit a Maestro selector. First inspect the recorded screen, accessibility hierarchy, emulator/Simulator logs, backend fixture and authoritative payment state; classify **script defect / test-environment mismatch / native application defect / provider failure** before selecting the least-cost repair.

**Verified failure and correction:** GitHub [run 40559070209](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/actions/runs/40559070209) reached the Android emulator but failed sign-in because `tapOn: "Password"` hit the static PASSWORD label rather than the editable field. Captured screenshot showed blank password and disabled Sign In; an Android Enter key was not proof of sign-in. Prior iPhone screenshot likewise showed blank password and a foreground sign-in overlay, contrary to the initial claim that a Traveler had reached destination selection. In QA branch [PR #383](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/pull/383), merged-on-branch [PR #384](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/pull/384) corrected BOTH flows to target the actual `Your password` input, assert enabled `auth-primary-action`, tap the real button, and wait for the login overlay to disappear. Add a cheap prebuild script guard before any expensive device job.

**Build policy:** `scripts/native-build-fingerprint.cjs` fingerprints tracked native sources plus relevant app-build environment; `.github/workflows/native-commercial-end-to-end.yml` restores an exactly matching macOS iOS simulator binary or Android APK when available, otherwise builds once, checksums and saves it BEFORE any Maestro or Stripe TEST device test, with separate retained binary artifacts. Changes only to `.maestro`, native-QA test helpers or documentation must reuse the same binary **within the accessible GitHub Actions cache scope**; confirm actual cache-hit and artifact availability rather than assuming successful reuse. Native source, environment or credential-relevant output changes justify a rebuild, never an unchanged UI locator. Both Apple and Google commercial signed store packages require their own subsequent qualification; unsigned simulator app and QA APK are NOT TestFlight/Play publication artifacts. GitHub PR cache scope may prevent reuse across independently numbered PRs: apply test-only patches to the SAME native QA PR after cached binaries exist.

Android was compiling its first uncached APK; iOS was booting simulator/building first uncached app. No preserved `commercial-qa-android-app` or `commercial-qa-ios-simulator-app` artifacts existed at the time of the check, and neither corrected paid Traveler→Operator journey had passed. The older iOS build from run 40559070209 was cancelled in favor of the corrected single campaign. ## 26 October 2026 | ACCOUNT DELETION IAM POST-CREATE AUDIT CORRECTED

**Founder Cloud Shell physical evidence, 9:23–9:25 PM Eastern:** The controlled account-deletion IAM script confirmed the exact `ar-production-live-runtime@american-rider-35688.iam.gserviceaccount.com` service identity; it reported no bound custom role, created custom project role `americanRiderAuthAccountDelete`, then stopped with an apparent role conflict. Founder independently ran `gcloud iam roles describe` and confirmed the role is GA with exactly one included permission, `firebaseauth.users.delete`. This distinguishes a successfully created role from the still unverified role binding.

**Precise source defect:** `gcloud iam roles list` returns summary metadata that may omit `includedPermissions`. The original script compared the missing field to the required permission and falsely aborted after creating the correct role. The narrow fix calls `gcloud iam roles describe` for the existing role and tests the real summary-only response; it keeps exact-project, actual Cloud Run service-account, and least-privilege fail-closed checks. Script remains read-only by default, explicit `--apply` performs the one missing binding only, and no app build, paid transport, Stripe, scheduler or Firebase user data is changed.

**Live remaining step:** Run the fixed, exact-commit IAM repair in authenticated Founder Cloud Shell. Do not claim deletion works until Cloud Shell confirms the bound role and a fresh disposable account can be removed in Build 56. If deletion still fails after binding, consult Cloud Run request logs before broadening any permissions.

## 24 October 2026 | OPERATOR RECRUITER SINGLE-CONNECT FINANCE (PR #322 REVIEW, MONEY OFF)

**Problem, actual evidence:** PR #321 MERGED at `8687d27bab780f97d46ecd8c11e0ff5c21fe1681` after 8/8 CI green and proved a separately approved Recruiter who is already an Operator can reuse their `users/{uid}.stripeAccountId` without creating a second Stripe Connect account. However, that PR ONLY provided read-only verification, not actual commission transfer/bank reconciliation. Prior live protected production deployment is b578f05, and PR #321/322 are NOT yet production-deployed.

**PR #322 proposed code-level completion, NOT commercial activation:** `backend/recruiter-operator-transfers.js` implements gated Stripe Connect **Transfer** of separately funded Recruiter reward batches into the Operator's existing Stripe balance using strong UID/account/approval/tax evidence; never creates a second Stripe Account or a separate bank Payout, and never changes the existing Operator automatic payout schedule. A new `AR_RECRUITER_OPERATOR_SHARED_PAYOUT_ENABLED` gate defaults OFF. The signed existing Recruiter bank status may bind the verified Operator recipient ONLY when approved Recruiter contract/tax clearance and independent money flags are present. The existing reward reservation ledger applies **zero additional $2 connected-account fee** in this shared mode, preserving existing Operator monthly fee billing and 99% of Fare. Transfer creates exactly one immutable attempt, checks provider transfer-group history and available platform funds, and fails closed on uncertain results. It records transfer as **operator_balance_pending**, NOT paid.

**Automatic bank proof:** Stripe's connected-account automatic payout is aggregate; `payout.paid` by itself does NOT establish a particular commission deposited. The new handler consults the indexed `recruiter_commission_payouts` records by exact `recipientRef/status` and only reconciles the commission when Stripe shows a PAID automatic payout with completed reconciliation and a payout-filtered balance transaction whose source matches the exact transfer `destination_payment` ID. `payout.failed/canceled` after payout is flagged for reconciliation, not silently reissued or clawed from other Operator Fares. Bounded queries; no global scans, no extra reads when finance feature is OFF. Composite indexes `recipientRef,status` and `recipientRef,providerPaymentId` were added to firestore.indexes.json and **must be deployed and ready** before enabling this feature. Existing independent Recruiter payout mode remains untouched.

**Synthetic acceptance required:** Assert money gates off; no recipient/amount/id forgery, no transfer with insufficient funds, idempotent one Transfer, no Stripe Payout create or Connect account update, no second fee; plain payout.paid without transaction inclusion cannot settle, exact automatic-balance-transaction proof can settle once, duplicate event cannot pay twice, later failed bank transfer requires review and cannot release available funds. Exact-head CI and test harness must pass before merge. Sandbox test connected account currently `type:none` and available USD -120 cents (pending +2240) and does NOT meet real Express KYC, so do not claim sandbox-funded bank transfer. Sandbox can validate behavior but not impersonate real KYC.

**Remaining LIVE external and code commissioning:** Final lawyer/tax ratification and accepted versioned agreement; human KYC/Stripe hosted bank details; real recipient with taxReady review; explicit founder money enable; actual funding (live Stripe connected recipients count ZERO and available platform balance ZERO at check); add `payout.reconciliation_completed` event to the existing connected-account webhook only after code is deployed and ready; deploy new Firestore indexes; protected current-SHA Cloud Run deployment; staged permitted LIVE transfer, Stripe bank payout confirmation and failed-return checks. No changes to routine direct paid Traveler/Operator booking and no second account or fee. Avoid calling simulated activity real.

## 24 October 2026 | FOUNDER SIMPLIFICATION: OPERATOR INVITES ARE NOT INDEPENDENT RECRUITER WORKSPACES (SUPERSEDES EARLIER OPERATOR DASHBOARD PLAN)

**Settled interface decision:** An ordinary Operator already has `Invite an Operator` and `Invite a Traveler` in the existing Operator menu. Preserve those two controls and the same sign-in. **Do not** add a Recruiter dashboard, charts, QR studio, recruit-count page, extra login, separate payout setup, extra Stripe Connect account, or second $2 active-account fee to the Operator app. They are not necessary to share an invitation. Independent full-time Recruiters keep the separately built, optional website portal and private analytics.

**What this PR actually does:** The server returns a signed `compensationActive` boolean with the existing role-specific invitation link from already-read approved identity/contract records. Only confirmed financially active paid recruiters include the FTC material-compensation disclosure in the native Operator Share. Ordinary unpaid invites remain an ordinary short message. This PR removes the earlier added link from Operator Invite to the Recruiter dashboard. On separately approved hosted Recruiter bank onboarding, a **single new** point read of `users/{uid}.stripeAccountId` rejects a second account for any Operator who already has Stripe Connect; no second account, no payout schedule alteration. No read/write added to normal Travel or Operator sharing beyond existing referral launch gates. Automated test asserts the rejection and zero Stripe create calls.

**Important external/payment distinction:** Although Stripe supports transferring other authorized funds to the Operator's existing account, the current dedicated Recruiter money pipeline requires a separate Express account and manual payout schedule. We did NOT secretly redirect Recruiter commission payouts to the existing Operator account: that requires a **separately tested single-account ledger/transfer/payout and tax classification**, with no double fee, approved terms, refund recovery, and no modification to ordinary Operator earnings/payout timing. Thus a current Operator who chooses *paid* recruiting will not be asked to open a second account; paid Recruiter bank enrollment remains blocked until a compliant shared-rail solution is separately commissioned. This does not inhibit ordinary referrals, direct paid Travels or Operator payouts.

**Email judgment vs source requirements:** One opt-in welcome/confirmation email after a newly introduced Operator is actually verified could replace dashboards, if desired, using existing Resend delivery. This is a future courtesy, **NOT** a launch condition; do not send emails on every share/click or every Travel, reveal another person's personal data, or claim email notifications are implemented. A sign-up / code used is not an independently verified qualified Operator or paid Travel. No extra messages or billing systems have been introduced.

**Published guidance vs Council judgment:** Apple HIG simplicity and least setup (<https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/design-principles/>), FTC straightforward material-connection disclosure (<https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/ftcs-endorsement-guides-what-people-are-asking>), Stripe Connect per-active-account and per-payout pricing (<https://stripe.com/connect/pricing>) and reusable connected-account transfer architecture (<https://docs.stripe.com/api/transfers/create>) are real external publications. Ford/Jobs/Rockefeller/Emirates are strategic lenses, not individuals personally consulted and none endorsed a particular screen. Our *judgment* is that Operators do not need Recruiter-facing analytics or a second Stripe account; that exact product choice is not mandated by Apple, Stripe or regulators.

Last verified protected Cloud Run production b5f8756; release/current b3b42f3 after merged PR #319. Do not conflate them or block direct Travels for optional recruiting.

**FOUNDERS' NEW EXPRESS FINANCIAL DECISION (supersedes all previous claims that the amount is illustrative or unapproved):** An approved independent Recruiter may earn **$0.15 per actually qualifying, completed, captured-and-Operator-settled Travel** generated by an attributed Operator; likewise **$0.15 per qualifying Travel** generated by an attributed Traveler. Never turn on finance flags simply because tests pass. Ordinary Operator/Traveler paths remain direct and fully independent of optional recruiting.

**Express Founder decision, superseding the former rate uncertainty:** A Founder-approved Recruiter earns **15 cents for each actually eligible and funded completed/settled paid Travel for a properly attributed Operator**, plus **15 cents for each eligible/funded completed/settled paid Travel for an independently properly attributed Traveler**. When both roles on one Travel have eligible paid Recruiters, reserve at most 30 cents, never deduct from the Operator's fixed 99% Fare share. Neither ordinary Traveler nor Operator signup or Travel needs any Recruiter or referral. A recruiter earns nothing for page views, QR scans, installs, invitations alone, uncompleted/unpaid Travels or refunded/disputed Travels. The prior model's `200 qualifying Travels / $30 per recruited person` is RETAINED only as a conservative coded safeguard, **NOT a separately ratified perpetual contractual cap**. The remaining payout minimum, hold/expiry windows, overall recruiting budget, and final independent contractor agreement require explicit actual written operational/legal approval; do not advertise those as final.

**Scoped source engineering IN THIS BRANCH, NOT YET CERTIFIED/DEPLOYED:** Set explicit versioned approved 15-cent `PROGRAM` at `enabled:false` until regulatory/provider/terms commissioning. Make immutable paid-referral intent available only on actual Founder-approved, agreement/identity-verified Recruiter claims, with no extra clicks or scans stored and no retroactive conversion of ordinary personal invitations. At booking, a **zero-Firestore-read** snapshot uses existing server-authoritative fare, platform fee, toll, tax/government charges, and card-country-cost model to reserve 30 cents only if **both potential rewards**, a small bank fee allowance, and the established 75-cent modeled contribution minimum are funded without changing the Traveler's agreed Total or the Operator's 99%. A Travel without sufficient contribution has no funded reward; these are conditional qualifying commissions, NOT an unconditional bonus. This quote model is not a verified final tax/accounting profitability statement.

The existing single leased production scheduler (no second cron) adds HELD events exactly once, matures held commissions after the separately approved `AR_RECRUITER_REWARD_HOLD_MS` and verifies continuing approval, batches available events from an indexed role/account query under an externally approved `AR_RECRUITER_MIN_PAYOUT_CENTS`, then reuses the existing fail-closed Stripe Express payout and provider-signed payout reconciliation. The fee remains $2 deducted once only in an active bank-payout month, processing bank-payout charges are platform expense, bank payment is never treated as completed merely because Stripe transfer was submitted. All new money/queue features require the separate opt-in `AR_RECRUITER_REWARDS_ENABLED=1`, existing legal/provider/link/program/paid-payout gates, an approved hold time and approved minimum; no activation secrets, user tax/bank data or actual Stripe money are changed by this branch. These are workload ceilings, NOT claimed usual request counts or actual billed reads; minimum/index/query billing and latency must be measured in Google/Firebase usage. Do not presume H3 is cheaper: H3 is a useful alternative for cell aggregation, not proof of better dispatch cost. - `backend/founder-platform-counts.js` performs ten on-demand Firestore indexed `count()` aggregations on explicit Founder request, no periodic full-user read or new analytics database. `backend/qualification.js` rate-limits unchanged qualification snapshot writes to once per 6h; `backend/operator-onboarding-start.js` uses a one-time transaction to avoid false fresh enrollments, which does incur a read plus a write on market selection. Target costly high-frequency dispatch lookup and operator duty heartbeat first *if measured expensive*.

## 23 October 2026 | NO-SHOW RELEASE GATE AND HONEST OPERATOR PAYMENT STATUS — SOURCE REVIEW

**Traveler-facing extension:** Updated the active Travel cancellation confirmation to avoid unverified flat $3 promises based on arrival stage alone. The receipt/refund follows immutable booking terms, and the app clarifies that the actual fee is verified by the server. The new text is translated into English, Spanish, French, Italian and German and checked by the existing native/source cross-role contract. This is source-only and requires a new signed mobile build.\n\n**Additional cancellation/legacy-fee audit, 23 October:** The separate *older* captured-card cancellation fallback could withhold an unconditional $3 whenever the status said `arrived`, even with `AR_VERIFIED_ENROUTE_CANCEL_FEE` OFF. This contradicted the commissioning gate and permitted an unverified charge. Updated `backend/travelmoney.js` to freeze the original independently verified fee or zero at cancellation, reject retroactive paid no-show handling of legacy/unconsented Travels, and treat preexisting unresolved cancellations without immutable evidence as fee-free. Targeted adversarial tests now cover arrival-only, concurrent arrival, refund provider outage, policy changes between retries, historical records lacking frozen evidence, and legacy/undisclosed no-show attempts. Prior *recorded* verified fee amounts remain frozen through retries. No live fee has been enabled. Review legal disclosures and any historical refunds separately.\n\n**New independent native/backend discrepancy:** The signed-app Operator pickup source shows a five-minute "Traveler Did Not Arrive" control regardless of whether the paid `/travel/no-show` API is commissioned. The backend correctly refuses requests unless `AR_VERIFIED_NO_SHOW`, `AR_MANUAL_TRAVEL_CAPTURE`, and `AR_MANUAL_CANCELLATION_FEE` are explicitly true. This leads to an apparently usable control whose action returns 503. A second discrepancy: after a valid $4 capture, the API returned no separate Operator-compensation pending field, so the native UI stated payment reconciled even if the $3 Connect transfer was still owed.

**Additional finding in Traveler cancellation copy:** For manual card holds, the arrived-state Traveler screen asserted a possible $4 fee even when the backend's fee switches were OFF or the original booking did not contain verified fee consent. Source now refers to the actual terms accepted when booking rather than implying every arrival creates a fee, with matching translations and a source-contract regression.

**Fourth defect, legacy captured-fare cancellation:** The old `paymentCapturePolicy` path still took a $3 arrival deduction with no verified fee policy, even while discretionary fee flags were OFF; this was independent of the newer, guarded $4 manual-hold flow. In this branch, the legacy captured-path fee now requires verified commissioned progress evidence, freezes the originally determined $3-or-$0 amount at first cancellation, and fails closed at zero for older cancelled Travels without a frozen fee decision. Synthetic tests cover an Operator arriving during the first cancel read, a Stripe refund outage and subsequent flag change, and an unverified historical retry. This change is source-only and does not turn on legacy or manual fees.

**Safe source-only repair:** Extend the existing `/config` response with `verifiedNoShowEnabled`, true only when all three server flags authorize the fee; parse it fail-closed in the existing payment configuration provider. Show the five-minute Operator control only while the actual server capability is verified; retain existing Operator withdrawal independently. After a valid manual capture, return `operatorPaymentPending` separately from Traveler `refundPending` and display a clear status, translated into all five current languages. New and extended synthetic/source-contract tests verify disabled/unknown/inconsistent configuration, real pre-existing server gate, payout success/failure, and native control wiring.

**Activation limits:** This change does not turn fees on. Advance fee display, versioned acceptance, published binding Terms, qualified legal review, actual sandbox Stripe captures/transfers, real GPS, physical signed iPhone/Android devices, and independently confirmed serving Cloud Run revision remain separate. The intended economics are $4 Traveler gross/$3 Operator net only for verified eligibility. No live money or provider settings changed. Source requires exact-head CI, review/merge and guarded deployment followed by signed app release proof.

The manual cancellation path cleared `refundPending` and could leave `arrivalFeePending` without any scheduled recovery: `sweepBookingRecovery` queried `refundPending` but not compensation-only obligations. The no-show and manual fee features remain default OFF; this is a pre-activation safeguard, not an observed live loss.

Treat a provider exception as a pending obligation, not as cancellation reversal. Add bounded, cursor-based `arrivalFeePending` recovery using the existing booked-Travel recovery sweep and the existing canonical Traveler/PaymentIntent + Travel ID + Stripe destination transfer guard. Only cancelled `manual_v1` Travels with frozen original $4 fee and $3 Operator net qualify; stale or forged flags cannot trigger a transfer. The source-only tests exercise a missing recipient, provider exception, resumed transfer, concurrent already-settled confirmation clearing a stale pending marker, and repeated sweeps without double payment. ## 23 October 2026 | OPERATOR NO-SHOW TRAVELER PAYMENT OWNER AND PUBLIC CANCELLATION AUDIT — SOURCE REPAIR

**New cross-role synthetic finding:** `/travel/no-show` allows a verified Operator to initiate a paid-Travel cancellation (only under separately commissioned, normally OFF no-show and cancellation-fee flags), but `backend/travelmoney.js` passed the *Operator caller uid* as `expectedUid` into `transferFixed`. A captured PaymentIntent is always owned by the *Traveler*; the fixed-fee Stripe provider guard rejects this mismatched owner and would leave eligible $3 Operator compensation unsettled even after a valid $4 capture. The proposed one-line owner fix binds the transfer to `currentRide.travelerUid`, preserving exact Travel ID, actual Operator Stripe recipient, Stripe verification, idempotent payout and all existing location/contact/no-show checks. The existing two-role no-show regression now asserts the originally charged Traveler identity on initial and repeated attempts. No no-show/fee feature has been turned on and no money has moved.

**Separate public contract mismatch:** The active public source pages `backend/site.js` /operate, /support and /travel and `backend/legal.js` Terms section 4 still describe an unconditional $3 arrival charge; /travel also says no money is charged before Operator assignment while current manual payments authorize a *hold* before dispatch. The modern *optional* fee design would charge $4 gross for $3 Operator net, only with advance disclosure and qualifying evidence; the fee feature defaults OFF. Existing website PR #265 corrects some old company-site claims but does not yet correct these cancellation/authorization passages. A focused review comment was added to PR #265 requesting correct website and legal text plus tests. This source fix does **not** rewrite binding legal terms or activate fees. Distinguish this pending copy work from the code correction.

**Release state:** Source branch `fix/no-show-traveler-payment-identity-20261023` requires its exact-head CI and reviewed merge; prior production Google Cloud Run revision `00025-wns` from `3e415ca` is independently verified. Do not claim this later code is live before a guarded existing-service deploy. Real signed-device and law/insurance checks remain separate.

## 22 October 2026 | VERIFIED EMERGENCY TRAVEL, MONITORED CASE REUSE AND LOCATION SAFETY

**Audit confirmed three server-side safety gaps:** (1) `POST /emergency` previously accepted a client's Travel Number, Operator name, vehicle and plate without checking they belonged to that signed-in person; a mistaken or forged client record could become dispatch-facing Emergency evidence. (2) Route monitoring may already have filed an Emergency under the Traveler's UID, yet tapping its push opened the screen with a fresh random request ID and created an unnecessary second case. (3) `updateTicketLocation` allowed the owner of an ordinary Support record to overwrite its `emergency` data and allowed impossible latitude/longitude and previously resolved Emergency cases.

**Guarded source proposal:** `backend/emergency-travel-context.js` reads the canonical Travel and checks this UID against its Traveler, Operator, Teen and guardian members. Emergency and vehicle identity come **only** from that verified server record; an absent/inaccessible/unavailable Travel cannot block 911 or urgent general case filing, but its fields cannot be invented. The new authenticated `GET /emergency/context` serves minimal role-aware facts and validates an existing monitored incident against the actual Emergency ticket before reusing it. `POST /emergency` reuses that ticket where confirmed, even if the Operator receives the alert, rather than filing a duplicate; ordinary cases remain idempotent via their stable per-screen request IDs. `src/boot/FullRoot.tsx` forwards only the authenticated push's Travel lookup hint, and the existing role-aware Emergency screen from merged PR #244 remains intact and refreshes canonical facts after a cold start. Call 911 stays an immediate manual button. `backend/tickets.js` now rejects non-Emergency, resolved-case, impossible GPS and empty location updates.

**Synthetic acceptance:** `backend/emergency-authenticated-travel.test.js` checks two-role ownership, Teen/guardian membership, denial of unrelated Travel IDs, validated incident reuse, absence of unsupported operator/plate details, and 911 manual-choice preservation. `backend/tickets.idempotency.test.js` adds tests for support-case GPS contamination, closed cases and invalid GPS. No emergency call, push, email, live ticket or payment created by this source work.

**Release state:** Prepared on `fix/emergency-verified-case-20261022`, **not merged, deployed or signed** at the time of this entry. Require exact-head CI, reviewed merge, guarded Cloud Run release, signed two-role TestFlight verification and simulated monitoring/incident delivery. [Issue #240](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/240) stays open until actual phone restoration and incident reconciliation are proven. [Issue #235](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/235) remains the independent unprotected-branch defect. Never claim provider delivery from synthetic acceptance.

## 22 October 2026 | EMERGENCY SHOWS CURRENT OPERATOR TRAVEL, NOT TRAVELER CACHE

**Verified source defect:** `app/emergency.tsx` reads only `RideContext.matchedOp` and `RideContext.lastTrip`. When the signed-in person is an Operator, their active assigned Travel is in `OperatorContext.op` and their current vehicle identity is `OperatorContext.vehicle`. The Emergency screen can therefore omit the Travel Number, Operator identity and plate during a real Operator emergency, even though this information is locally known.

**Guarded source correction:** `src/emergencyTravelFacts.ts` selects current real Travel facts by authenticated app role. For Operators it demands both a real `rideId` and `tripNo` from the live Operator context; for Travelers it requires a currently active Travel with a matched Operator. It does **not** substitute previous trips, demonstration vehicles, unknown amounts, or blank vehicle fields. `app/emergency.tsx` now displays and attaches these role-correct facts to the existing idempotent emergency case, and shares the current route to trusted contacts. New two-role source/runtime checks: `backend/operator-emergency-facts.test.js`. No phone call, emergency ticket or provider action executed by this PR.

**Limits:** During a fresh app cold start, before the Operator context has restored, the screen still files the urgent request immediately and may initially lack vehicle details. **Issue #240 remains open** for server-authoritative cold-start identity and monitored emergency duplicate-case reconciliation. Do not weaken immediate emergency notification or falsely mark that issue resolved. Require exact-head CI, guarded merge, signed-device proof, simulated case filing and two-role physical verification.

## 22 October 2026 | FAIR OPERATOR PAYOUT QUEUE — SOURCE CORRECTION

**Confirmed source defect:** The old `backend/scheduler.js::sweepSettlements` read only `needsPayout=true` with `.limit(25)` and no cursor. When 25 legitimate blocked debts remained at the front, later eligible Operators were not reached on subsequent minute sweeps. This is a verified infinite-starvation path, not proof any real Operator has already been underpaid. A blocked Connect account must remain a recorded debt; deleting or auto-refunding an earned amount is not acceptable.

**Guarded source repair:** `backend/payout-fair-page.js` reads at most 25 outstanding records per sweep by indexed Firestore document-ID order, starting after the last checkpoint in privileged `scheduler_state/operator_payout_page`. A document-ID checkpoint persists only after the page's capture, transfer, and reconciliation logic finishes. When the cursor reaches the end, the next page starts from the beginning. A failed checkpoint may repeat work; the existing provider-backed `payout-transfer-guard.js` prevents double payments. No deletion, silent forgiveness, account changes, fare/toll/99% modification, Stripe secret changes or new paid vendor. Adds one privileged cursor read and at most one cursor write each minute in addition to up to 25 payout-query reads. Firestore document ID sort avoids a new composite index.

**Synthetic acceptance:** `backend/settle.test.js` now models bounded Firestore `orderBy/startAfter/limit`, persistent cursor, 25 blocked debts followed by 15 eligible paid Travels, later inserts, safe wrap, owed flags and prevention of duplicate payouts. This is simulated Stripe and Firestore; actual Connect bank funding, financial provider failures, cross-instance timing and production data remain separate commissioning evidence.

**Historical state superseded:** PR #243 is **merged** with all seven exact-head CI campaigns passed, including twice-exhaustive, as verified above. A paid Cloud Run deployment and provider settlement verification remain open. Open payout issue [#241](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/241) must not be closed until all safeguards are verified. Source authority at start: `release/current` `c1359c8793f9ccb868cdc44fa280f2ce2090ca0b`; production runtime is separate.

## 22 October 2026 | EMERGENCY GPS TRUTH UNDER MOVEMENT — SOURCE CORRECTION

**Verified source defect:** `app/emergency.tsx` previously kept the previous street name while updating the GPS coordinates and "Updated" clock. If the vehicle moved to another street within the ten-second geocoding interval, the old address could appear current. A slow `reverseGeocodeAsync` result also used to replace the entire location record with the older lookup's coordinates and timestamp after newer GPS fixes. Incorrect emergency location is materially worse than displaying verified coordinates without a street name.

**Guarded repair:** `src/emergencyPosition.ts` holds pure, testable state rules: the latest GPS sample timestamp wins, late old samples never roll back location, a previous street label survives only within a 15-meter neighborhood of its verified coordinates, and delayed reverse-geocoder responses may update an address only when near the **current** point without replacing current coordinates or timestamp. `app/emergency.tsx` uses these rules with device `l.timestamp`. `backend/emergency-position-truth.test.js` covers 145m movement, sensor reversal, invalid coordinates and slow geocoder. The 911 button, incident reporting, actual emergency ticket storage, existing protected permissions, Operator status and Stripe money are unchanged.

**Historical state superseded:** PR #242 is **merged**, with all seven CI campaigns passed. A signed iPhone build and physical Emergency position acceptance remain open. Require exact-head CI, guarded merge, new signed TestFlight and physical walking/driving location validation with simulated/stubbed emergency case; never place a real 911 call. Separate Operator role information and duplicate remote emergency-case behavior remain open in [#240](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/240).

## 22 October 2026 | AUTHENTICATED NOTIFICATION DESTINATIONS — SOURCE CORRECTION

**Confirmed source defect:** Existing backend publishers deliver live push payloads to `/emergency` (monitor emergency), `/operator/trip` (Operator stationary check-in), `/wallet` (scheduled-payment/consent problems) and `/` (unmatched scheduled Travel). `src/boot/FullRoot.tsx` recognizes only five routes and silently ignores those four legitimate, existing destinations. Separately, the guardian check-in from `backend/monitor.js` incorrectly linked to `/ride`, which reads a Traveler's own session, rather than the Family supervision view. This breaks actionable alerts even if Expo accepts a push.

**Proposed correction:** Explicitly admit those four legitimate native routes while still checking authenticated `recipientUid === user.uid`, strict route allowlisting, and notification-only navigation (never payment, duty, acceptance or 911 auto-dial). Send guardian monitored-Travel check-ins to `/family`. Completed Travel notification opens `/history` instead of a cold-start `/receipt` with no selected Travel. Add `backend/push-route-parity.test.js` to compare all static publisher destinations with the actual available native routes and preserve recipient/cold-start guards. Update existing cold-start source regression to allow multiline allowlist formatting. **Deeper audit of Operator offers found a second confirmed defect:** immediate dispatch, scheduled dispatch, announcement and assignment sweeps stamped `notifiedOperatorAt` even when Expo refused the push. The isolated source fix stamps only when a valid Expo acceptance ticket is returned. Failed push stays visible in the live Operator inbox during the normal 45-second answer window and passes to ordinary replacement/stranded handling after timeout. `backend/operator-push-acceptance-marker.test.js` and additional full-behavior `backend/monitor.test.js` cases preserve this rule. **Expo accepting a ticket is not proof a notification reached the handset.** No push delivery is falsely certified: physical iPhone/Android, cold-start, background and emergency/guardian tests remain separately required.

**Further confirmed audit correction:** Immediate and scheduled Operator assignment push sends the private four-digit Teen PIN in notification text. This can expose the pickup secret in lock-screen previews. The guarded source fix now sends a neutral instruction without the PIN; the same PIN remains accessible only by authenticated Teen/guardian through the existing protected API, with a synthetic privacy regression. Reassignment push also now respects the original 45-second answer deadline even if Expo only accepts a notification late, avoiding a false fresh offer. Provider-acceptance is still not device delivery. Separate open issues [#240](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/240) (Operator emergency identity/duplicate cases) and [#241](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/241) (blocked-payout pagination fairness) remain uncorrected pending their own safe tests.

**Teen private-code recovery verified in source:** Removing the four-digit PIN from a push also exposed a separate discoverability defect: a Teen notified on their own account did not have the booking loaded in that account's ordinary `/ride` context, because the guardian may be the booking owner. New `app/teen-pickup.tsx` reads a validated Travel ID only after authenticated recipient-bound notification navigation, calls the existing protected `/travel/teen-pickup/code` endpoint, shows the PIN only inside the signed-in app on explicit request, and rejects stale responses from an older Travel. Immediate and scheduled Teen notices now target this route. The existing guardian Family Travel code and server Teen boarding verification are unchanged. Both new tests enforce protected code routing and non-disclosure. This is source-only until green CI and signed-device Teen+guardian checks.

**Historical state superseded:** PR #239 is **merged**, with all eight CI campaigns passed. A new signed native release and real notification acceptance remain open. Final release must be checked by exact PR SHA, signed binary and actual delivered/tapped pushes. Does not send push, create tickets, dial 911, change payment, touch Operator eligibility, or add an external vendor.

**Independent further audit:** Payout settlement scan in `backend/scheduler.js` reads the first 25 `needsPayout=true` documents without a moving cursor. If these remain legitimately blocked, later payable Travels can be starved; require separate provider-safe paging/fairness test before altering live money flow. The operator-side Emergency screen also currently obtains Travel/vehicle identity only from `RideContext` (Traveler state), so Operator-initiated emergency metadata must be tested independently. These are recorded as open investigations, not claimed fixed here.

## 22 October 2026 | OPERATOR PAYOUT STATUS OUTAGE TRUTH (PR #234; SOURCE UNDER TEST, NOT DEPLOYED)

**Verified defect:** The production `GET /connect/status` route returned HTTP 200 `exists:false` when the Stripe key or Firestore was unavailable. The provider lookup also returned `exists:false,error` after Stripe failures, and the native helper treated all non-2xx responses as no Connect account. An Operator with an existing payout account could therefore be invited to start onboarding again, give fee consent or believe the account disappeared. On the same screen, Focus + initial mount produced duplicated provider reads. The manual status recheck could redraw identically without acknowledging a successful unchanged response.

Tested against genuine absent/account-ready, provider 502/503, legacy false-empty, malformed response, offline and nonterminating request stand-ins; 27 isolated prelaunch suites expected after CI, including a new whole-app literal-navigation destination audit. This does not create or modify Stripe accounts, alter settled money, relax screening/insurance or represent a real connected Operator as available.

## 22 October 2026 | ACCEPTED OPERATOR PREBOARDING WITHDRAWAL AND SAME-TRAVEL REPLACEMENT (SOURCE PR, UNDER TEST)

**Prior launch-wide trace PR #225 MERGED** with seven exact-head GitHub checks successful. This proposed follow-up has NOT been merged or deployed to paid Cloud Run or signed apps.

**Scope of one ordinary failure:** After accepting, before a Traveler boards (including after arrival), the assigned Operator sometimes cannot collect them. On the existing Operator pickup screen, the Operator deliberately chooses **Unable to Complete Pickup** and confirms. Only the authenticated server, not the phone, atomically returns the **same** travel to released/assigned, excludes the withdrawing Operator, marks them off duty, clears their reservation, preserves the original Travel Fare, Platform Fee, trip number and **original Stripe PaymentIntent**, and discards stale Teen/adult proof. It cannot be used on onboard, completed or cancelled Travels. The change is safely idempotent and may be retried after a dropped response. The Traveler (and Teen guardian, when applicable) receive an essential push that a new Operator is being sought; the existing single leased assignment re-offer sweep retries bounded geohash eligible candidates. If none is available in a **five-minute** window, the existing same-Travel cancellation, provider refund/authorization release recovery, and cancellation notification apply. No second booking, fee, bank transfer, speculative refund or independent scheduled worker is created.

**Unchanged:** Production backend is **paid Google Cloud Run**; prior Render web/preview status cannot prove backend revision. One Founder, existing dispatch/payment ledger and Firestore; adult verbal name + vehicle plate and Teen four-digit code; no extra infrastructure, no paid vendor, no new index, no customer needed for synthetic tests. Never claim Stripe bank settlement, signed-device wallet/3DS or store publication from this source PR.

**Verification gates:** `backend/operator-withdraw.test.js` is an isolated adversarial suite testing preboarding status, identity, price and payment immutability, no double withdrawal, Teen PIN reset, no after-boarding withdrawal, route/notification/UI wiring, all five locale keys. Integrated into existing `scripts/prelaunch-synthetic-acceptance.cjs` running in PR Release Gate and before separately founder-authorized builds. Keep source, CI, deployment, signed distribution and external real-world compliance separate. Existing Ops/support remains the emergency path for an already onboard Traveler.

## 22 October 2026 | PR #226 UNDER TEST: accepted Operator cannot complete preboarding pickup

**Founder authorized a single exceptional-Operator safety path and requested #225 merge.** PR #225 has **merged with seven of seven checks green**, merge `6ba78f3`; source changes are not independent signed-device or Cloud Run deployment evidence. The existing Operator money display and active-only inbox improvements remain intact.

**PR #226 SOURCE UNDER CI, NOT YET MERGED OR DEPLOYED:** An Operator who accepted a Travel but cannot collect the Traveler may expressly withdraw before boarding, in either `accepted` or `arrived`, through the authenticated `/travel/operator-withdraw` endpoint. The server uses one atomic transaction to release the **SAME** Travel, retain its original payment and quoted amount, exclude the withdrawing Operator from replacement, clear previous Teen/adult proof, take the Operator off duty and release the reserved vehicle slot. It never creates another PaymentIntent, captures money or marks a completed journey. A duplicate request returns unchanged; another Operator or an onboard/completed caller is refused. The action requires a deliberate confirmation on the pickup screen and success from the server before the app clears the operation.

**Traveler notification and automatic recovery:** Push notice to Traveler and separate Teen guardian is required; Traveler's existing live ride listener shows searching while the existing 60-second assignment sweep attempts fully qualified replacement. If no qualified Operator takes it within **five minutes after withdrawal**, the existing sweep marks the Travel cancelled and `refundPending` for the existing payment/authorization recovery; sends a final cancellation notice. The original payment can be verified, voided, or refunded by the **existing** idempotent payment-recovery worker. There is no extra platform/no-show cancellation fee or departing Operator settlement for a journey not provided. A missing phone push ticket never undoes the transaction. Source and synthetic tests do not prove eventual device push delivery or provider banking.

**No new cloud resources or changes to live paid Cloud Run:** no new Scheduler, database index, subscription, H3 migration, Firestore-wide poll, Stripe charge, EAS or TestFlight, production deployment or public store release. No adult PIN; Teen proof remains server enforced. This is a source-only candidate. Require exact-head CI before merge. Independent legal, real-world settlement and store acceptance remain separate.

**Two source-only fixes in this PR (not yet CI/merged/device-deployed):** (1) Operator Complete formerly computed platform commission as `Fare - Operator total`, but Operator total includes 100% toll reimbursement. Example $17.80 Fare + $2.75 toll: Operator gets $20.38 and true platform commission is $0.17, NOT a negative $2.58. Correct the screen with validated cent arithmetic, separate toll row, five-locale estimated-earnings label, and no claim that Stripe already transferred funds; carry server toll cents to completed local record. (2) Operator inbox formerly listened to all historical rides for its `operatorId`. Live Google index READY state not independently read.

**Single prelaunch synthetic gate:** Expanded existing isolated release-gate runner from nine to 20 deterministic suites, covering the complete journey, onboarding, money/payout, schedules, support and failures. Existing workflow and manual build guards remain. Source tests are not payment/network/phone attestation. Critical remaining workflow gap identified: accepted Operator cannot safely withdraw via an authoritative cancellation/reassignment path (`cancelOp` goes offline but does not release accepted Travel, by design); retain existing Support emergency escalation and require tested server lifecycle before adding a control, not a misleading local cancel. Optional penalties OFF, Smart Travel disabled, no adult PIN, no new infrastructure, no production deployment or signed distribution.

**Standards:** Google SRE/Firestore bounded indexed reads, Stripe idempotency and Apple truthful feedback. Florida §627.748(7)(d) independently requires qualified counsel/insurer evaluation before real paid carriage; no claim of legal approval.

## 21 October 2026 | TEST card capture succeeded; cancellation release accounting correction (source candidate)

**Authenticated Stripe TEST acceptance progressed on GitHub run #39708135079, attempt 2, at `cabb3167`:** Github's updated TEST key successfully authenticated to the connected Stripe sandbox (`acct_1U5AJVAZW1pAcE2d`). `balance.livemode=false` preflight passed. Actual TEST PaymentIntent `pi_3UTBPxAZW1pAcE2d01YLBgYr` was manually authorized for $10 and captured $10 (provider status `succeeded`, amount_received 1000). `pi_3UTBPyAZW1pAcE2d2gGOqWFv` was manually authorized for $10 and canceled (provider status `canceled`, amount_received 0, amount_capturable 0). The canceled uncaptured Charge has `captured=false`, `amount_captured=0`, and no balance transaction. Stripe automatically created a Refund *record* for released authorization (`re_3UTBPyAZW1pAcE2d2FzHmMHu`) with **no refund balance transaction**. This is not evidence of captured or settled funds. Stripe's cancel API expressly states an uncaptured authorization's remaining amount is automatically refunded. Reference: https://docs.stripe.com/api/payment_intents/cancel

**Source correction:** The test incorrectly required zero Refund objects for an uncaptured authorization; instead assert zero captured amount, no original charge balance transaction and no refund balance transaction, alongside status canceled. Preserve explicit TEST-key verification and cleanup. The initial run was correctly red because this was a test-assertion mismatch, *not* an authentication, live payments or customer refund problem. A separate freshly dispatched manual TEST workflow must still pass all three cases, including the untested $4 partial capture, before calling provider acceptance complete. ## 21 October 2026 | Stripe sandbox acceptance key format correction (source candidate)

**Provider test not yet certified.** Manual workflow run 39673669538 (founder, `release/current` `b96140b`) passed GitHub checkout, configured secret/prefix and Stripe SDK installation, then failed before any Stripe API request. Root cause: the workflow accepted any `sk_test_*` prefix, but the Node script rejected characters outside `[A-Za-z0-9]` following that prefix; Stripe's published API authentication contract establishes `sk_test_` for test-secret keys, not that narrow suffix alphabet. The source candidate permits a nonblank `sk_test_` value with optional outer copy/paste whitespace, rejects all live/restricted/public credentials and embedded whitespace, and makes a **read-only Stripe balance request** to confirm authenticated `livemode === false` **before any test-only authorization**. No API key is printed, the production secret is never passed, and the original test-mode full capture / no-fee void / partial capture assertions remain unchanged.

This corrects validation only: it is not evidence that the saved GitHub secret is a complete, valid Stripe secret. If the provider returns an authentication error, the Founder must replace the encrypted GitHub `STRIPE_TEST_SECRET_KEY` with a complete actual Stripe standard test secret, never paste it into chat, and rerun the workflow with `STRIPE_TEST_ONLY`. Manual capture and the optional $4/$3 policy are still disabled. Cloud Run revision `00013-4wl` remains the last source-verified live revision `b96140b`; any newer release requires its own controlled source-only deploy.

Source: https://docs.stripe.com/api/authentication ; https://docs.stripe.com/api/balance/balance_object

## 21 October 2026 | End-to-end Traveler/Operator lifecycle and pickup-proof review

**FOUNDER REQUIREMENT:** End-to-end completeness must be verified across quote, payment, dispatch, eligibility, accept, arrive, boarded, destination, completion, refund, payout, receipt, scheduled cancellation, support and recovered failures. Follow Apple/Google design/engineering, Lyft/Uber real ride procedures, Google's reliable systems and Stripe official payments guidance; do not claim outside counsel/institutions personally approved implementation. Dedicated matrix: [21 Oct end-to-end journey completeness](docs/audits/2026-10-21-end-to-end-journey-completeness.md).

**Already established (do not rebuild):** Operator `confirmArrival()` calls server GPS-gated `arrived`; `beginTrip()` asks the server for `onboard`; `completeOp()` requests a GPS-gated `completed`. On real paid Trips, server-side transitions are strict: accepted -> arrived -> onboard -> completed. An Operator's tap without server validation cannot release payout. Mandatory Teen code; optional six-digit adult code with keyed private delivery, five-attempt lockout and current assigned Operator. Completed record, settlement sweep, Stripe Connect transfer, receipt and Traveler log code paths exist, along with cancellation/refund recovery. This is a source finding, **not** confirmation that real payment or operator/human travel happened.

**Material gap found:** `ADULT_PICKUP_VERIFICATION` is OFF by default. When enabled, the existing code enrolled adult verification only if the Traveler proactively requested the code, leaving a race in which a dishonest Operator could assert `onboard` before enrollment. **Source-only proposed fix:** under the *same pre-existing flag*, atomically enroll the adult code at **acceptance** within the server's existing Travel update, while keeping old clients and production rollout unchanged. An enabled but missing secret must reject acceptance rather than silently bypass identity proof. Code still not universally active until Founder separately authorizes coordinated compatible-device rollout. No extra Firestore read/write in acceptance. Teen verification unchanged.

**Synthetic extension:** Continuous eligible Operator acceptance, assigned-vs-accepted, adult PIN acceptance enrollment, false boarding refusal and private code, wrong Operator, proper Teen pickup, strict GPS arrived/completed, no payout before completed, exactly-once transfer and receipt, Stripe-provider-down cancellation with durable owed refund, recovered and repeated refund, and declined offer reoffered under same paid Travel id. Inspect these results in current-commit CI. Do not claim this uses real Stripe, phone GPS, insurer or device.

**Further gaps / separate gates:** One-mile destination radius and Operator-stamped GPS do not prove a human actually reached the exact destination; preserve Traveler safety/dispute path. Long-term cancellation-history UI absent from bounded scheduled list. Aged paid `needs_attention` cases may disappear from compact Home before resolution. Real-time network/background/iPhone/iPad, independent Operator acceptance, actual Stripe refund/payout/receipt/webhook, accessibility and permanent support case resolution need genuine matching evidence before commercial completeness. Prefer event-driven reconciliation over extra Firestore polling. **No TestFlight/EAS distribution without new, explicit Founder authorization per release; no live backend or Stripe changes from this audit.**
