## 21 October 2026 | Founder TestFlight authorization hold + cancelled reservation and dispatch-state audit

**BINDING FOUNDER RELEASE DIRECTIVE (21 Oct 2026): Do not create, trigger, or submit ANY new TestFlight or EAS distribution build unless the Founder has given NEW, explicit permission for that specific release.** Earlier broad launch/test instructions do not authorize further TestFlights. Source-only GitHub PRs, ordinary CI unit/integration tests, and local/CI bundle exports are permitted but not signed TestFlight distribution. Do not infer permission from merged PRs, healthy build checks, or a desire to launch. No TestFlight requested, built, or submitted in this review.

**Physical device update:** Founder screenshots at 08:58–09:00 local show American Rider opening normally with a scheduled Travel and quote, the no-Operator result screen, and a separate Scheduled Travel detail accurately saying **Reservation cancelled**. This is physical evidence of a running application following previous PR #196; screenshots alone do not prove which signed build number is installed, full paid-booking qualification, or a complete crash-free release. Existing cancellation record must remain accessible; status on Home must be accurate and not misleading. Do not assume two screenshots minutes apart represent simultaneous database state.

**Confirmed source defect:** `fetchScheduledRide` previously selected a cancelled reservation while its `atMs` remained later than current time minus 60 minutes, and Home always displayed the card under "Upcoming Travel" regardless of `schedState.status`. The Firestore watcher updated `schedState` but did not clear `scheduled/schedInfo`; a cancellation from `/upcoming` also did not synchronously clear that context. A 07:15 appointment scheduled for tomorrow could consequently appear on Home until approximately 08:15 tomorrow, even if cancelled today. The same bug could confuse other terminal/error statuses. The server transaction correctly marks cancellation only if status is `reserved`, and the dedicated history lists it correctly.

**Source-only proposed repair (this review):** Exclude cancelled reservations from bounded Home restoration, dismiss them on authoritative real-time snapshot or verified `DELETE` success, and immediately reconcile the current Home context after list cancellation. Preserve the cancelled server record; the existing Scheduled Travel UI is a bounded near-term list and excludes records more than 90 minutes after scheduled pickup. A dedicated long-term cancellation-history view is not yet demonstrated. Name operator assigned/no supply/payment failure/needs attention/price change accurately in Home. Distinguish no-Operator terminal/manual-retry state from "Still arranging," show no En Route step before verified operator acceptance; never invent ETA, payment success or Operator status. On a verified no-Operator result before payment, explicitly state **No charge was made**; for paid or unconfirmed payments, preserve the existing separate recovery/refund status. Apply Apple 44pt minimum touch targets to the retry/change/return actions. Preserve existing server-authoritative dispatch, idempotent retry, and payment/refund gates. No additional polling, Firestore listener, payment change, Stripe mutation, fee change, deployment, or automatic TestFlight.

**Reference standards consulted for this material UX/operational review:** Apple HIG Feedback, Progress indicators, Alerts (https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/feedback ; https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/progress-indicators); Google Firebase onSnapshot official semantics (https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/query-data/listen); Google Cloud idempotency (https://cloud.google.com/discover/idempotency); NN/g system status heuristic (https://www.nngroup.com/articles/visibility-system-status/); Uber developer ride-request lifecycle and terminal no-driver / redistribution states (https://developer.uber.com/docs/riders/ride-requests/tutorials/api/best-practices). For **future material interaction, status, booking or payment changes**, compare relevant authoritative platform guidance and respected HCI/software/service-engineering institutions, explain where American Rider intentionally differs, and record evidence and tested transitions before release. Search is evidence gathering, never a substitute for physically verified business logic.

**Additional discovered coverage gap:** A scheduled reservation with \`needs_attention\` (payment charged but Travel not created) currently ages out of Home after roughly 60 minutes past pickup and the near-term Scheduled Travel list after roughly 90 minutes. The backend record persists, but perpetual visibility, support escalation, and refund reconciliation are not certified; address independently before launch. Do not mistake an absent card for successful payment resolution.

**Further audit gates:** Check cold/warm restart, cancel from both Home and Scheduled Travel, listener replay/race with cancellation, same-id exact-once retries, operator assigned vs accepted vs arrived vs onboard vs completed, decline/reoffer/expired response, no-supply with paid/unpaid/unknown payment, refund/failed refund, cancelled vs completed in receipts/log, app background resume, network loss, and VoiceOver labels. Do not report all of those as physically accepted without matching evidence. Keep backend production and payouts unchanged.

## 21 October 2026 | P0 physical iPhone crash: confirmed scheduled-total restore mismatch (PR #196)

**Actual signed iPhone diagnostic evidence:** After Builds 48/49 terminated before the home screen, the Founder installed diagnostic Build 50 (`cd5b5d9f`) over the existing app and supplied the on-device screen: `AR-BOOT · TypeError`, `Cannot read property 'toLocaleString' of undefined`. This proves a JavaScript exception is visible to the PR #195 boundary; it does NOT independently prove native-only crashes or make Build 50 a repair.

**Reproducible source-level cause:** `backend/server.js` writes scheduled reservations with authoritative `costCents: priced.travelerPays` (positive integer cents), **not a `cost` field**. The in-memory initial POST return in `src/backend/scheduled.ts` reconstructs `cost`, so the booking initially appears correct. But on cold launch `fetchScheduledRide()` retrieves the unaltered Firestore document and `src/state/RideContext.tsx` previously copied `cost: r.cost` (undefined) to `schedInfo`. `app/index.tsx` rendered `fmt(ride.schedInfo.cost)`; `src/theme.ts` invokes `n.toLocaleString()` without an unknown-value guard. This matches the exact error observed and requires no new provider, polling, or database migration. Build 47 was known-good on the founder's iPhone; source history between 47/48 is still text-only, so **the date the incompatible reservation was created/loaded is not independently established**.

**Proposed limited repair in PR #196 (source only):** Pure `scheduledDisplayTotal` restores the saved integer cents / 100, honors finite positive legacy-dollar records only when cents are unavailable, and returns `null` if both are missing/invalid. Home conditionally shows the existing translated unavailable-quote message and never calls the money formatter on unknown; Upcoming uses the same decoder. `ScheduledRide.cost` is correctly optional for raw Firestore documents, while a new booking requires a valid positive quote. Synthetic behavioral test `backend/scheduled-boot-price.test.js` reproduces the server's `costCents`-only record and covers missing, malformed, and legacy prices. No money, production backend, Firestore reads/writes, payout or scheduling cadence changes.

**Release gate:** Finish CI, merge verified repair, produce one exact-SHA signed TestFlight, and verify actual startup with the Founder's existing scheduled record on iOS 18.4.1 before considering App Review or marking issue #192 resolved. PR #195 is diagnostic containment only. Do not claim full real-device resolution yet.

## 21 Oct 2026 | PR #193, Build 48 native startup isolation candidate

Physical iPhone **Build 48** (`e6a6a6a`) crashes on launch (user-provided actual Apple .ips, `SIGABRT`/RCTExceptionsManager fatal JavaScript). Native JavaScript error **not yet recovered**. `release/current=b02a0b45` has green JS source gates and paid Cloud Run deployed but has **not** been qualified crash-free on an actual device. Source review exposed an independently confirmed startup failure mode: root `app/_layout.tsx` imports `RideContext`, which imports `src/backend/liveActivity.ts`, which eagerly constructs the optional Expo widget via `src/widgets/TravelActivity.tsx` at module evaluation time. The widget source explicitly records an earlier `ArgumentCastException` at startup. An exception thrown before the call-time try/catch can kill the entire app. PR #193 isolates this *optional* widget until ActivityKit is actually needed, and contains its exception inside a guarded lazy import. It also moves constants and helper functions used by the serialized 'widget' layout **inside** the marked function, as required by Expo SDK 57's isolated widget runtime, preserving displayed wording. Adds a behavioral regression that compiles real TypeScript, injects a throwing widget, and checks that startup still succeeds and healthy start/update/end behavior remains. **Credible protection only, not proof that it resolves Build 48.** Direct Xcode simulator diagnostic is being evaluated separately; prior Expo simulator runner failed during Apple data migration and `devicectl-exists` (environment error). Do not submit another TestFlight without the Founder's authorization. No live payment, production deployment, or main release change from PR #193. See [P0 #192](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/192).

Includes synthetic routing regression and preserves geohash dispatch. Cloud Run and physical booking validation remain separate.

## PAYMENT-METHOD JOURNEY REVIEW | 20 OCT 2026 | PR #186

**Founder requirement:** Verify the Traveler's payment-method journey. Synthetic provider-mocked backend/payment-method-journey.test.js exercises overlapping first-time Customer requests, canceled SetupIntent, saved card, default switch, ownership checks, remove default, remove last, and Apple/Google wallet wiring. No live money or physical-device operation is claimed.

The $2.50 floor remains and the fee increases as required by modeled costs. **Final provider cadence:** Founder approves **weekly automatic Standard bank payouts on FRIDAY** plus eligible **Instant Payouts throughout the week**. This is not a new Operator payday/calendar feature. `backend/connect-payout-schedule.js` now defaults to Stripe `interval=weekly,weekly_anchor=friday` for newly onboarded connected accounts; it updates and rechecks Stripe before stating completion. `CONNECT_STANDARD_PAYOUT_INTERVAL=daily` remains emergency rollback only. Stripe's separate two-day settlement/availability delay is unchanged. Previously created connected accounts require explicit migration; latest live account query returned ZERO connected Operators, so none needs retroactive migration today. User's platform account weekly schedule is not to be changed.

Do not hold the first commercial launch waiting for a historical Stripe fee invoice that cannot exist. Existing versioned Operator consent covers only actual separately billed fees. Allow paid travel and the Operator's 99% gross entitlement now, retain a durable future recovery obligation when a genuine provider report arrives; never charge an estimate, fees already paid directly by an Operator, or a Traveler's saved card. The per-Travel earnings offset implementation in PR #183 remains `CONNECT_VERIFIED_FEE_RECOVERY`-gated and needs final Firestore composite index deployment and production billing reconciliation before setting the fee money-moving flag; this is a transaction-safety control, NOT a regional/booking gate. Operator presence freshness, screening, insurance and SOS may never be traded for quota savings. ## STRIPE LIVE FEE EVIDENCE REVIEW | 20 OCT 2026 | OPERATOR FEE DEDUCTIONS STILL DISABLED

**Stripe connected account live state checked through authenticated Stripe plugin:** platform account `acct_1U5AJMAF5hzJigJI` had **zero** connected accounts returned by `GET /v1/accounts` and no Stripe fee balance transactions or payouts returned. The sandbox account has one Express account and sample `controller.fees.payer=application`, automatic daily schedule with 2-day delay. A sandbox fee payer is *not* proof of the future live account's commercial price plan. Existing contracted operator terms require actual fees; no actual fees are chargeable now.

**Source correction:** Stripe's All Fees report at https://docs.stripe.com/reports/all-fees is the appropriate starting point for provider fee evidence, not an estimated price. Its itemized report type `all_fees.balance_transaction_created.itemized.2` includes `fee_transaction_id`, `incurred_by`, `product`, `feature_name`, `amount`, `tax`, `currency` and `settled_via`. The report may take up to 96 hours to include fees after they affect a balance, and excludes some products that Stripe invoices after service. **Monthly totals cannot automatically be charged to each Operator without row-level provider attribution and settled billing proof**. New `backend/connect-fee-reconciliation.js` and tests parse trusted itemized reports and quarantine absent payout IDs, postpaid invoices, fee credits, unknown currencies and unclassifiable fees. Report parsing does **not** activate Operator debits or claim that a CSV is automatically authenticated; the run itself and each payout-to-account mapping need separate verification. Data ingestion, report downloads, payout attribution, credits, exact bank remittance and Stripe pricing agreement are still commissioning gaps.

**Stripe reporting availability authenticated:** Stripe's `itemized_fees` and `connected_account_itemized_fees` reporting schemas include `connected_account_id`, `fee_transaction_id`, `incurred_by`, `settled_via`, fee amount/tax, etc., but querying Sigma was refused because the live Stripe account **does not have a Sigma plan**. Do not subscribe merely to make this work; no paid Sigma is needed for the principal implementation. Prepared `backend/connect-fee-report-run.js` (and tests) to create/poll `all_fees.balance_transaction_created.itemized.2` reports via Stripe's standard Reporting API instead, using 96-hour mature windows. The CSV authenticated retrieval + completed-run linkage, each unique connected-account/payout attribution, exact collection and compensation for later fee credits remain unimplemented and must fail closed. Published plans and fees can be checked in https://dashboard.stripe.com/settings/plans-and-fees. Stripe's existing live account itself does not expose a contract-wide Connect pricing choice before a real connected account is created.

**Instant Pay research:** Stripe Express Instant Payout eligibility depends on the connected account's status, onboarding history, destination support, enabled controls, positive instant-available balance, and risk/daily limits. Common usage does not guarantee every new account immediate access; Express Dashboard shows the option if available, but platform may need to initiate payout if disabled in Dashboard. References https://support.stripe.com/express/questions/instant-payouts-for-express-accounts ; https://docs.stripe.com/connect/instant-payouts . Operator UI should only advertise eligibility conditionally, no extra forced card for fee recovery.

**Two Operator payout experiences remain final:** automatic Standard to bank + eligible self-initiated Instant in Stripe; no Operator calendar, additional fee-card, manual-only payout or extra Stripe charge on Traveler card. Research: https://docs.stripe.com/connect/manage-payout-schedule ; https://docs.stripe.com/connect/instant-payouts ; https://stripe.com/connect/pricing .

**Engineering added in stacked PR #183:** `backend/connect-payout-schedule.js` validates explicit `CONNECT_STANDARD_PAYOUT_INTERVAL=weekly` + `CONNECT_STANDARD_PAYOUT_WEEKDAY=<weekday>` and adjusts the connected account's weekly anchor using Stripe only when configured; no flag preserves existing Stripe schedule. `daily` also supported as an explicit rollback. PR #182 is now MERGED into release/current, removing unsafe old Traveler-card charges and historical full scan. Stacked #183 remains a draft with provider-ID payout activity and due-only review, not yet payment netting. Authentic Stripe LIVE list was empty as of this review; no live fee records or Connect payouts existed. Sandbox Express account `acct_1UJoynAZW1TLcJBR` showed `controller.fees.payer=application`, enabled payouts and automatic daily / 2-day delay; this does NOT establish future LIVE price contract or amount billed. Stripe bills many platform-managed Connect costs monthly in aggregate; published tariffs are **estimates, not sufficient proof** to deduct an Operator's balance. Settlement must verify actual account-specific charges, allocation, fee payer and consent, then atomically offset future transfer with a once-only ledger and refund/reversal handling; if fee evidence is absent, no fee debit. No new card or Stripe account-debit extra fee by default. Do not claim actual recovery is live or first paid Operator launch is commissioned.
 
**Verified fee-offset implementation now prepared in same DRAFT #183:** `backend/connect-fee-offset-plan.js` + `backend/verified-connect-fee-transfer.js` and tests now enforce matching Operator/Stripe account, preexisting versioned consent, actual provider balance-transaction ID, application fee payer, explicit provider evidence, one claim per provider fee, partial carry-forward, and a positive net transfer. The existing `payout-transfer-guard.js` now has transactionally reserved obligations, immutable gross / net accounting, Stripe provider transfer amount comparison, and fee finalization with same transfer, followed by provider reconciliation on uncertain results. **Default: `CONNECT_VERIFIED_FEE_RECOVERY` unset = zero deduction and no query.** Only a deliberate `CONNECT_VERIFIED_FEE_RECOVERY=enabled` activates this money path once the exact Stripe provider-fee importer, monthly invoice-to-Operator allocation, reversal credits, index deployment, legal review and live-like signed-device sandbox proof are commissioned. Stripe billing cannot yet be imported automatically because live account has no connected Operators and no billed Connect fees. Never fake evidence flags to force payment. ## Verification and payout-dashboard repair | 20 October 2026 | DRAFT PR #183 ONLY, NOT DEPLOYED

## FINAL FOUNDER DECISION | 20 OCT 2026 | OPERATOR PAYOUT UX: STANDARD AUTOMATIC + OPTIONAL INSTANT ONLY

**Permanent product scope:** American Rider offers Operators ONLY **Standard payout (automatically sent to their bank by Stripe)** and **Instant payout (Operator-selected IF Stripe eligible, additional disclosed fee)**. There will be **NO American Rider payout-date calendar, Friday/weekly/monthly choices, manual-standard payout-request button, or separate required Operator fee-payment card**. The Stripe connected account still has an internal automatic frequency (default daily unless changed by Stripe/risk/country); that is back-end provider processing, not an Operator product feature. Stripe's transfer of 99% earned Travel Fare into the connected account on completion is distinct from the later ordinary bank deposit. Existing `/connect/dashboard` opens Express; actual Instant availability in that hosted experience must be confirmed in a TEST account. Standard does not add 1% instant *surcharge*, but its underlying Connect bank payout and routing fees may apply depending on actual pricing owner; do NOT claim it is universally free.

**User research supports simple choice:** Everee survey May 2025 of 419 U.S. gig drivers reports 70% prefer daily OR instant pay (not a universal payout-timing measurement); Visa's Postmates case reports 70% of deposits used instant and 63% of active couriers used it, contextual rather than predictive; NBER 2024 randomized Uber trial finds offering Instant Pay increased labor supply compared with weekly. Source refs https://www.everee.com/blog/gig-report/ , https://corporate.visa.com/en/products/visa-direct/resources/case-studies/postmates.html , https://www.nber.org/papers/w33177 . No justification to add a weekly-calendar product.

**Gross versus net:** Operator **earns** 99% of Fare gross, plus applicable toll reimbursement. After separately itemized and accepted actual applicable Connect costs, *net deposited amount may be lower*; American Rider pays Traveler card processing. Merged consent #152/#165 stands, but PR #181 mandatory separate fee card is closed unmerged. **DRAFT PR #182** disables unsafe old monthly collection via saved Traveler card, avoids previous-month ALL-account six-hour rereads, preserves signed Stripe payout activity webhook, and adds read-only, provider-ID-deduplicated fee scenario tests. It now presents **Standard/Instant** plain language on Operator payout screen and clarifies gross-vs-net disclosures in all five translations. This is **NOT merged/deployed** and does not yet collect fees from earnings; source/policy must never be described as revenue-recovered.

**Chosen collection principle:** Operator fees should come from consented earnings where possible, NOT an obligatory second card. Stripe can directly bill a connected account in certain pricing configurations; its balance Account Debits require binding consent, eligibility and incur extra 1.5%, making them an expensive default; alternatively, carefully itemize/offset **actual already accrued** fee obligations against later Operator transfers while leaving the contractual gross 99% Fare share intact, with exact payout-ID and calendar-month accounting. Do not create advance $2 charge before an actual qualifying bank payout, do not charge a failed/canceled payout as paid, and do not double-charge Stripe-handled direct fees. Stripe fee payer for our existing Express/separate-charges-and-transfers integration must be independently confirmed. https://stripe.com/connect/pricing ; https://docs.stripe.com/connect/account-debits ; https://docs.stripe.com/connect/separate-charges-and-transfers .

**Google/peer-engineering & cost guard:** Current six-hour previous-month fee sweep could reread all accounts up to 120 times over 30 days while that month is being processed; safe PR #182 disables it while money recovery is not approved. Before reenabling, use webhook-driven due obligations, index-backed page limit and provider-idempotent exact-once ledger; preserve event queue and SOS/safety checks. Google recommends index-exemption discipline and Query Explain; Uber H3 is geospatial analytics and needs an actual reads/writes/latency benchmark vs existing geohash. https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/best-practices ; https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/query-data/explain ; https://h3geo.org/docs/comparisons/geohash/ . NO H3 migration or index delete without measurements. FTC worker fee-transparency action https://www.ftc.gov/legal-library/browse/cases-proceedings/handy-technologies supports clear Operator fee consent and truthful pay claims. Historical Council remains review rubric; **no direct retained counsel was consulted or approved**. See [detailed design and evidence](docs/audits/OPERATOR-EARNINGS-FIRST-PAYOUT-SIMPLIFICATION-2026-10-20.md).

**Release status:** Draft PR #182 is source, not live or actual first Operator paid booking; full fee recovery, Stripe pricing owner verification, provider payout webhook reversals, exact bank remittance and sandbox+signed TestFlight commissioning remain blocking before claiming Operator-paid Connect economics are operational.

## Founder's corrected Operator-payout decision and self-audit | 20 October 2026 | NEW SAFE SOURCE PR ONLY

**CORRECTION TO PREVIOUS ADVICE:** PR #181's mandatory *second Operator card* was excessive and has been CLOSED UNMERGED. No Operators are yet enrolled, so protect the first Operator by onboarding safely rather than creating unrelated credit-card fees. **Founder preference: recover expressly authorized actual Connect charges from Operator's earnings, not Traveler-saved cards nor an extra Operator card by default.** The already-merged $2 payout-active month fee and versioned Operator consent (#152/#165/#170) stand. Do NOT promise the $2 is due when a completed Travel merely transfers funds to Stripe: the $2 is only triggered by an eligible bank/debit payout in that month. Operator receives **99% gross Fare entitlement**, separately itemized actual fee obligations and net proceeds; no unauthorized changes to that 99% or Traveler payment.

**PAYOUT TIMING IS NOT A FOUR-FRIDAY PRODUCT RULE.** `transferToOperator()` moves earned funds into the connected Stripe Express balance when each Travel completes. Stripe then distributes bank payouts: **automatic ordinary payout schedule normally daily unless the platform deliberately changes it; optional Instant only upon eligible Operator election**, generally 1% additional. Bank payout timing is distinct from time money reaches the Connect balance. Our source has no Friday/week/month bank payout creation and `/connect/dashboard` delegates balance visibility to Stripe. Manual-only ordinary payouts would require a new self-service payout control, NOT advised unless Founder later requests it. Stripe docs: https://docs.stripe.com/connect/payouts-connected-accounts and https://docs.stripe.com/connect/manage-payout-schedule .

**NO FREE STANDARD CLAIM:** If platform handles pricing, Stripe's published US prices are $2 per bank-payout-active account/month + (0.25%+$0.25) per bank payout + separately 0.25% routing/platform management of bank payout volume; Instant +1%. Example one $100 standard payout in an active month = $2.75 costs; same instant = $3.75. The **other** Stripe-handled pricing model lists no extra ordinary payout/account/routing platform fees, but its compatibility with existing Express + separate charges/transfers and actual authenticated Stripe pricing owner is UNVERIFIED; do not automatically enable. Account debits from connected balance require legally binding consent and a separate 1.5% Stripe fee; another possibility is a separately agreed fee netting from next Travel transfer, accurately accounting 99% gross then Operator fees. Collect fees ONCE on actual provider occurrence, never an estimated monthly cost. Source https://stripe.com/connect/pricing and https://docs.stripe.com/connect/account-debits .

**Discovered actual unsafe behavior:** Current `backend/payments.js:chargeOperatorAccountFee` uses `customerForTraveler()`/saved Traveler card for the Operator $2 recovery, despite no independent mandate. Monthly `operatorFees` also rereads ALL previous-month accounts every six hours, unnecessarily billing Firestore reads. New safety PR **fails closed on such off-session Traveler-card charges** and disables monthly card sweeps while leaving signed payout event recording in place; a small read-only synthetic fee preview tests payout counts, month deduplication, Standard vs Instant and collected amount boundaries. **NOT A COMPLETE FEE RECOVERY:** payout-ID itemization, actual pricing owner, authorized earnings offsets, $2 and per-payout fee collection, failure/reversal reconciliation and signed-device sandbox acceptance remain to implement before public paid Operator onboarding. Do not deploy the safety PR as a complete paid-launch settlement solution. The fare engine conservatively reserves modeled Connect expense until reimbursement proven.

**Next engineering and reads/writes:** Commission provider pricing/payment-fee owner, itemized once-only obligation by Operator/payout ID and month, proper consented net earnings or connected-balance recovery, no extra card; test sandbox 0/1/many payouts, duplicate and failed events. Replace full six-hour historical monthly fee scans with webhook-triggered obligations + index on DUE/nextAttemptAt, bounded pages, retries with deterministic keys, only changed-state writes. Retain already merged PR #138/#159/#161 Firestore wins. See [detailed Stripe audit and sources](docs/audits/OPERATOR-EARNINGS-FIRST-PAYOUT-SIMPLIFICATION-2026-10-20.md). This is not a retained attorney opinion, no Stripe live fee/account state or money changed.

Neither live website nor email delivery from a deployed site has been proven here. Render hosts actual site statics, and a distinct web server remains provisioned; merge+auto-deploy+external HTTP/HTTPS verification are separate stages.

**Stripe canonical policy (DO NOT REOPEN):** A Traveler pays American Rider's STRIPE CARD PROCESSING; Operator is responsible for ALL applicable Connect bank-payout-account, bank payout, funds routing, and optional instant payout charges by explicit separate contract. Their **99% of Travel Fare remains gross entitlement**, with full actual toll reimbursement; never quietly take fees from that 99% without separately authorized compensation/fee mechanism. Under platform-handled Connect pricing (not necessarily the account's ACTUAL plan) published US rates are **$2 per connected account only for a calendar month containing an actual bank/debit-card PAYOUT**, **0.25% + $0.25 each ordinary bank payout**, **separate 0.25% of funds routed to banks**; Instant Payout has additional **1% for Connect** (distinct from public non-Connect 1.5%). Funds routing is **money movement/management and is NOT road directions**, nor a separate customer fare. Standard transfers are not free to the platform under that model, though operator should not pay extra expediency surcharge for noninstant. Source https://stripe.com/connect/pricing ; https://docs.stripe.com/connect/instant-payouts .

**Already merged:** PR #152 implements explicit monthly active account cost $2, NO old 20-Travel waiver; PR #165 enforces versioned Operator consent before NEW onboarding, new live duty/offer acceptance, and prevents nonconsented old Operators from consuming new paid offers; PR #170 preserves this. Consent BEFORE undertaking new paid work is required. HOWEVER, **previously earned Stripe transfers and bank payouts are never hostage to accepting new fees**; `/connect/dashboard` does not block existing earnings. Fee is assessed **only if a bank/debit payout really occurs in that month**, generally AFTER a provider bank payout event, not an advance non-refundable monthly subscription. No bank payout in month => NO $2 account charge. There is no general statutory/Stripe authorization to take earned money hostage for new billing terms.

**Not implemented or independently proven:** (1) a dedicated Operator-specific off-session card/bank/balance debit mandate separate from Traveler's saved payment method; current `chargeOperatorAccountFee` may call the saved Traveler payment method; this is not consent to recurring Operator fees, (2) robust stable monthly charge identity and Stripe webhook reconciliation when amount or timeout changes; (3) actual reconciliation and Operator recovery of 0.25%+25c per bank payout, 0.25% funds-routing and 1% instant fee, (4) actual account-managed pricing rather than public headline, and (5) actual successful LIVE operator Connect payout/fee collection. Read-only live Stripe account inspection at time of source review returned **zero connected accounts** for the connected live Stripe context; this does not prove all external accounts or the company's future Stripe use. **DO NOT switch live charging or hold earned payouts without explicit consent, sandbox tests, qualified counsel and confirmed actual fees**. Current `backend/economics.js` conservatively reserves about **15c** on a $17.50 Fare ($0.09 modeled percentage + $0.06 fixed Connect allowance) until reimbursement is actually proven; this is NOT a founder policy reversal, and cannot be removed speculatively.

**Clear release acceptance for Operator fee:**
1. Require separate clear Operator fee authority (fees + frequency + payment method), distinct from Traveler payments; independently test refusal/withdrawal/current fee version.
2. Verify actual Stripe Connect pricing configuration and chosen payer model; never double charge Stripe-handled direct pricing and platform-collected fees.
3. Reconcile provider bank-payout IDs to month, account, exact amount and pricing, one $2 fee per payout-active month, zero without bank payout, and same-ID idempotent retries even after 24 hours.
4. Collect/record each applicable ordinary, funds-routing and instant fee ONCE from the authorized Operator method, after provider fee occurrence; supply clear ledger/receipt, refund mistaken collections; never withhold already owed payout, never silently alter 99% contract.
5. Run Stripe SANDBOX end-to-end before live money and physical two-device testing; no live charge/payout from a GitHub documentation merge.

**HERE policy remains:** Keep HERE interim PRIMARY place/business autocomplete where private Photon is NOT commissioned, because current account supports the initial economics. Reserve HERE for TRUE exceptional unresolved toll calculation after OSM/OSRM/official verified data; OpenFreeMap renders visual map tiles and is NOT a toll tariff API. Read HERE invoice and actual per-SKU pricing/plan, sign off permitted TNC usage: HERE Base plan **excludes some asset-management/location vehicle tracking uses**; no assumption commercial right from $0 recurring Base plan. HERE per-instance failed-Photon 10/min throttle does NOT cap routine HERE-primary search; implement cross-instance/account-wide rate/spend controls and provider quotas WITHOUT disabling valid destinations. Re-evaluate Photon on measured economics; no Geoapify signup and no premature VM. HERE source https://www.here.com/get-started/pricing/base-plan-restrictions ; https://docs.here.com/policies/docs/limits-and-quotas . Outstanding full toll matching and operator verified SunPass profile are separate release safety work (#176, PR #177), and approved authoritative official toll books are empty; do NOT claim exceptional HERE-only toll behavior is commissioned just because policy is ratified.

**Source-only:** webpage text, canonical ledger and independently runnable regression tests; no real account, Stripe, Firebase, Render, Cloud Run, Apple/Google store mutation or lawyer communication performed. Historic Council names are a decision rubric, not actual counsel consultations.

## Verified en-route cancellation progress, no new recurring GPS database traffic | 2026-10-19

**FOUNDER RULE (already decided):** Two-minute free cancellation period after acceptance, then a disclosed $3 only when a responsible Traveler cancels after verified Operator progress or server-recorded arrival. Reuse existing geolocation evidence and avoid paid HERE routing/database polling. **New focused source PR** adds a conservative first-principles geometry proof in `backend/pickup-progress-fee.js`: reuse Operator snapshot ALREADY retrieved by `acceptOffer` in the same Travel update to establish baseline, and Operator snapshot ALREADY read in `cancelTravel` to release their slot. Requires two fresh, distinct timestamps, at least 0.20 mile and 15% pickup-distance closure, directional efficiency >=55%, plausible speed <=75mph, correct active Travel, and no operator fault or excessive lateness. Under missing evidence the fee is $0, rather than inventing progress. This is a **directional GPS proxy**, not a real road-route efficiency engine or absolute spoof-proof truth. No new external routing provider, GPS-history collection, reads, per-90s writes or subscription. Test thresholds are provisional policy parameters for counsel/field review, not observed competitor algorithms.

**SAFETY:** Current paid back end still captures at booking and retains existing arrived-only $3 behavior. New en-route mechanism is gated behind **OFF-by-default** `AR_VERIFIED_ENROUTE_CANCEL_FEE`; GitHub merge must not activate charges. ## Founder operating rule: platform readiness is separate from individual Operator qualification — 2026-10-19

**FINAL COMMERCIAL LAUNCH POLICY (supersedes earlier global screening-attestation launch blockers):** American Rider launches its first public iOS and Android applications with the capability to book and pay for Travels on day one. The **platform** is ready when the real Cloud Run API, Firebase, live Stripe payments, two signed Stripe webhooks, one authorized dispatcher, market controls, mobile clients and store approvals are operational. Publishing the app **does not require an existing fleet** or a pre-approved first Operator. Availability/matching must truthfully show no eligible Operator where none is available; do not complete a paid Travel without lawful eligible supply.

**EVERY OPERATOR IS REVIEWED INDIVIDUALLY:** Operators arrange and maintain insurance qualifying for their applicable jurisdiction and service, including the required commercial livery/TNC liability coverage, and submit their evidence for platform verification. They select their own lawful background-screening company; American Rider does not require an exclusive CRA. The Operator supplies company and case/report references, authorizes valid provider-to-platform delivery, and follows the jurisdiction's documented screening standards. An Operator's declaration, self-submitted report or software test is **never** automatic background clearance. Authorized review verifies the real source and required criminal, sex-offender and driving-history checks; documents, licenses, insurance, current screening and connected payout account must pass before the Operator can commence operations, receive or accept dispatch, or earn a payout. Lapsed/uncertain eligibility prevents duty/dispatch; required adverse-action procedures remain intact. No platform-wide money setting overrides those individual checks.

**TECHNICAL CORRECTION:** The former global `SCREENING_EVIDENCE_WORKFLOW_VERIFIED` environment variable remains an **informational external-provider evidence attestation**, never a fabricated approval and no longer a requirement for the payment API to be activated. A missing attestation cannot veto an otherwise operable platform when no unapproved Operator can be dispatched. Existing authenticated screening intake and Operations review remain; external provider evidence must be obtained and verified for each real Operator case. Synthetic tests exercise eligibility, insurance, bad reports, no-supply and dispatch denial; they cannot certify authentic consumer reports or regulatory compliance. Retain actual source evidence and lawful review for real cases. This founder-level policy supersedes historical comments elsewhere in this ledger demanding one exclusive provider or a blanket pre-launch screening pilot before paid platform activation.

**LIVE STATUS / NEXT EXECUTION:** The earlier GitHub workflow on 2026-10-19 at 11:47 UTC could inspect the actual Cloud Run Scheduler and Stripe version, leaving only the legacy screening attestation as a global code blocker. Correct the source and run CI/synthetic tests on an exact SHA, then use the founder-approved controlled Cloud Run ACTIVATE_PAID workflow (without setting an unverified attestation), independently verify public production payment readiness, transfer **existing** live Stripe account and Connect webhook destinations from Render to Google with unchanged signatures, disable Render sweeps, enable exactly one Google dispatcher and verify its state. Then certify signed native iOS/Android clients against Google production, submit store listings and secure Apple/Google approvals. No staging repeat, no copied secret, no second Stripe webhooks, no falsely approved Operators.

This overrides PR #100 and older ledger language asserting `GEOAPIFY_API_KEY` is a mandatory prerequisite for Cloud Run launch; historical prose retained as a dated record. [ADR 0038](docs/decisions/0038-provider-neutral-address-search.md). Provider costs/license and signed-device search completeness need commissioning; no Render, Cloud Run, Stripe, live mapping or deployment changes made.


**2026-10-18 OFFICIAL TOLL COST AND GEOAPIFY SCALING REVIEW (SOURCE, NOT LIVE):** Founder wants existing MapLibre/OpenFreeMap/OSM/OSRM/OTP preserved, Geoapify for address/POI autocomplete, and HERE exceptional for unresolved toll prices. OSM can tag `toll=yes` and `charge=*`, but volunteer price tags alone are not current official rates or proof every crossing is mapped. Current South Florida has no certified `tollCoverage` and no local verified rate catalogue; most routed toll quotes consequently still contact HERE, and a reliable HERE fraction cannot be claimed without route/official audits. New narrow source adds a fail-closed official toll rate gate requiring independently certified **every-crossing** identification, fresh approved fixed-rate authority book and correct two-axle/SunPass context; its actual approved catalogue is intentionally EMPTY, so this is not yet lower live HERE billing. Privacy-safe 1% sampled source decisions can be enabled in staging with `AR_TOLL_AUDIT_SAMPLE_PERCENT=1`; off by default, no coordinates/identifiers in records. Typing debounce grows from 180 to 350 ms for pickup and destination to reduce repeated Geoapify requests without disabling short-name search; network requests remain cancellable. Geoapify official published terms: 1 credit/autocomplete, $109/month 25k/day (15/sec), $609/month 250k/day (30/sec), custom quote above, 100k simultaneous Travels NOT 100k search requests/sec. At hypothetical 100k completed Travels/month with five billed queries/Travel, $109/month is $0.00109/Travel, not proof of full economics. Google toll estimates also incur premium charges; Uber H3 is spatial indexing, not toll pricing. Official Florida Turnpike/GMX rates, exact facility-route matches, variable lanes, vendor quotas, actual bill/usage/fallback %, signed devices and Google Cloud commissioning remain pending. Council review decision: [ADR 0037](docs/decisions/0037-official-tolls-and-destination-search-economics.md). No Render production or Cloud Run deploy, payment, provider configuration, index, app build or live fee change made. #98 remains launch authority.


**2026-10-18 CLOUD RUN PRIMARY PLACE-SEARCH (SOURCE, NOT LIVE):** The founder reaffirmed that HERE is a last-resort address-search provider. Previously, `backend/place-search.js` used HERE on all uncached autocomplete requests. This current-source candidate integrates primary Geoapify location/place autocomplete, request coalescing and ten-minute bounded caching, explicit exception-only HERE use after an actual primary failure (opt-in via `AR_HERE_AUTOCOMPLETE_FALLBACK=1`, disabled by default, 10 calls/minute PER INSTANCE), and localized Geoapify/OpenStreetMap/HERE attribution. A valid `GEOAPIFY_API_KEY` has NOT been verified in Cloud Run/Render. With no primary key the candidate returns no suggestions, so **DO NOT DEPLOY before commissioning** a primary provider and validating national POIs/addresses. Native geocode of entered text is not cross-platform business autocomplete. Per-instance fallback limits are not a fleet-wide cap under autoscaling; configure global vendor quotas when opting in. Secure the new key via Google Secret Manager and record terms/attribution and spend limits; no secret was created or copied in this source change. Existing Render live deployment and Stripe remain unchanged.


**2026-10-18 OPERATOR DIAGNOSTIC FULL-HISTORY READ CAP, ISSUE #95 (SOURCE ONLY):** Protected `/operator/me` could scan a particular authenticated Operator's entire historical `rides` collection just to show five recent statuses. Cap the response at 201 indexed records, and if more than 200 exist return a clear 409 incomplete-diagnostic response rather than an inaccurate complete total. Keeps own-account authentication, current eligibility and payouts unchanged; existing `rides(operatorId)` query requires no new composite index. Regression checks the hard limit and failure reporting. CI before source merge, no production deploy. Distinct GPS write cadence and inbox listener savings remain under issue #95 for later review.


**2026-10-18 REGULAR TRAVEL OPERATOR-SEARCH CANCEL VISIBILITY:** The actual founder complaint concerns `/ride` while the app searches for an Operator, not just Plan Ahead `/schedule`. PR #72 already merged server-authoritative cancel and uncertain-refund safety but the search Cancel control sat below a secondary safety block and could require scrolling. This source-only change places the *same* Cancel and confirmation immediately under the search result, makes the control at least 44 points and screen-reader-labeled, and preserves the server-authoritative cancellation, Return Home and Patron Support fallback. Adds a focused regression. This does not alter payment/refunds or automatically update an installed TestFlight build. Six exact-head CI campaigns before merge, no production deployment.


**2026-10-18 REMOVE REDUNDANT MOBILE SWEEP CALLS (SOURCE ONLY):** Current signed-in Operator app invokes GET `/scheduled/sweep` on a timer without the server-only token. The current backend deliberately returns 401; the paid Render process already runs authorized sweeps every 60 seconds. New narrow PR removes on-duty client wake-ups and leaves the old shared heartbeat exports as harmless no-ops. This reduces requests and mobile work without changing the actual Operator location/presence renewal, dispatch, pricing, schedule, or login. Synthetic regression verifies no unauthorized phone poll and intact server schedule/auth; exact-head CI before source merge. No Render/mobile production deploy.


**2026-10-18 PATRON SUPPORT HISTORY CURRENT-SOURCE PROPOSAL (NOT DEPLOYED):** New support history query orders cases newest first in Firestore, scans no more than 100 rows across pages, filters Operator-only cases, and conservatively warns about potentially incomplete history. Existing no-order query can omit the real newest cases. This change introduces composite `support_tickets(uid, createdAt DESC)` in the manifest; NOT VERIFIED READY in Firebase and MUST NOT be deployed until commissioned, with privacy tests and exact-head CI. Ported narrowly from old PR #78, which will be closed only after equivalent source is merged. No real cases, payment, deploy, credentials or production data altered.

**2026-10-18 PR #89 ZERO-DISTANCE PERMISSION RECHECK, SYNTHETIC REVIEW ONLY:** After six exact-head GitHub workflows passed on `afb26cc`, review found that a previously registered zero-distance background task could report success even after the Operator revoked permission. **2026-10-18 PARKED OPERATOR CROSS-PLATFORM PR #89 REVIEW UPDATE, NOT DEPLOYED:** Android's former 150 m location movement threshold also risks silence when an Operator is parked. **2026-10-18 STATIONARY OPERATOR PRESENCE, VERIFIED SOURCE VS DEVICE GATE (REVIEW ONLY):** Existing background TaskManager and foreground ~90s duty renewals, five-minute fail-closed dispatch, background location permissions/notice, and nearby geohash matching are BUILT. The original 150 m movement requirement risks silent presence expiry on both parked iPhones and Android phones. This PR proposes `distanceInterval:0` on both platforms while explicitly In Service, **reconfigures an existing persisted native task during an app upgrade only if permission remains granted**, rejects stale/poor/mock native fixes, bounds SUCCESSFULLY acknowledged background server renewals to one/90 sec while parked or at most one/30 sec after 300 m actual movement per running task, and shows existing location warning on foreground resumption without fresh GPS. Operating-system scheduling is not guaranteed; battery impact and stationary 20-minute locked TestFlight/Android acceptance are NOT yet demonstrated. Do not claim uninterrupted background duty or unlock new Travels without real fresh location. Server five-minute cutoff, insurance/screening, Stripe, location privacy stop-on-off-duty, geographic dispatch, and live deployment remain unchanged. Google geohash and Uber H3 support geographic candidate grouping but do not solve phone liveness; no H3 migration in this PR. **Staging project created:** `american-rider-staging-2026` (#926320586690) confirmed by founder's Google Cloud screenshot, distinct from Firebase production `american-rider-35688`; billing/project resources/Cloud Run staging service and connection are not yet verified. **2026-10-18 DUTY & INSURANCE VERIFIED TEST INVENTORY / OPERATOR NOTICE (DRAFT):** The five-minute dispatch freshness cutoff is AUTOMATIC and only excludes new assignments when server-accepted Operator `onlineAt` is too old; it does NOT end an ongoing Travel or suspend an account. Foreground renewals normally run ~90 seconds; native background location renews when permissions and iOS/Android scheduling permit. Five minutes is an engineering tolerance, not legislation. The existing In Service UI warned about unavailable background presence; this PR additionally shows existing `onlineError` for failed renewals without falsely declaring server status. Initial insurance upload or mere policy expiry is insufficient: independently confirmed active insurer/broker evidence is required; automated refresh is due at 30 days; at days 31–35 an Operator attestation is required to use limited grace until independent reconfirmation; after 35 days new dispatch is blocked. Reported lapse/cancellation/expired coverage blocks immediately, and no new carrier/CRA inquiry is made for each Travel. These behaviors already have comprehensive synthetic tests: `operator-presence-foreground.test.js`, `insurance-monitoring.test.js`, `insurance-monitoring-pagination.test.js` (620 simulated Operators), `synthetic-travel-lifecycle.test.js`, `synthetic-provider-chain.test.js`, and payment suites. **Binding maintenance instruction:** At the START of every American Rider repository task read this table and verify the GitHub and Render states before saying something is missing. When work is completed: update **this current-status table in the same PR or release follow-up**, with (i) built/code, (ii) merged commit, (iii) exact production deployment, (iv) App Store/TestFlight/Android artifact if applicable, (v) actual provider/market evidence and (vi) remaining specific gate. **Never convert review-only, CI-passed, 'green' staging or a standalone proposed PR into LIVE.** Once a feature is implemented, describe refinements as refinements, not feature creation. On each successful Render deployment, record the SHA; do not leave old 'Render blocked' status as the leading message. For major changes or verified external steps, update BOTH the newest current commissioning entry and the current-status table; use a new superseding ADR for a changed settled design. GitHub checks should flag missing ledger changes in protected critical files. This procedure applies to each future task; it does NOT claim that GitHub or this chat automatically monitors outside services in the background.

**Commercial launch certification remains UNVERIFIED.** Last source audit lacks demonstrated authorized live Operator insurance confirmation, approved CRA result workflow, qualified Florida legal review on platform obligations, protected live tri-county admission, controlled complete Stripe charge/refund/payout reconciliation and exact native-device acceptance for paid Travels. A server status of LIVE means *that version is running*, not that every market and payment path has passed external launch commissioning. No previously deployed feature should be redesigned merely to close those evidence gaps.

Existing draft PR #67 already covers GPS throttling, nearest-first matching and payout claims; draft #70 already implements bounded due-only Family age-out; do not redo. The new research in [READ-WRITE-CAPACITY-RESEARCH-2026-10-17.md](docs/audits/READ-WRITE-CAPACITY-RESEARCH-2026-10-17.md) prioritizes measuring estimated 7-read/1-write on-duty 90-second renewals, safe query inventory before any high-frequency GPS field index exemptions, full Travel listener impact and strict H3-vs-geohash parity benchmarks at equal coverage. Exempting a field from indexing may reduce index fanout and latency but DOES NOT eliminate the charged document write. Requires authenticated 19/19 live Firestore index evidence and real isolation before any schema or system change. No live database, money or TestFlight operations. Existing source regression for the two new indexes was relaxed to allow future approved indexes without dropping those two.**

**2026-10-17 TWO NEW FIRESTORE INDEXES — COMMISSIONING CANDIDATE, NOT YET LIVE-READY:** Prior authenticated commercial GitHub Actions run #39054270115 audited Firebase `american-rider-35688` and reported `Declared=17 READY=17 pending=0 missing=0` for the then-current 17 indexes. The separate new `rides(operatorId,status)` index from draft PR #67 bounds active Operator dispatch-inbox queries; `family_links(status,teenDob)` from draft PR #16 bounds due-Teen age-out checks. This **index-only** candidate adds both to the canonical manifest and source-integrity tests, without merging PR #16/#67 or deploying app/backend/charging funds. The connected GitHub API does not expose workflow dispatch. An index manifest commit alone does not create either live index. The founder explicitly requests ledger-first reuse of verified prior work. Research: [Google index usage and Query Explain](https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/query-explain), [Apple sheets exit/Cancel](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/sheets), [NASA intended-environment validation](https://swehb.nasa.gov/spaces/7150/pages/16449673/SWE-055%2B-%2BRequirements%2BValidation).**

**2026-10-16 OPERATOR INCOMING REQUEST EXPLICIT-DECLINE CORRECTION — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Audited actual Commercial `app/operator/index.tsx` request sheet and `src/state/OperatorContext.tsx`. Existing source renders Traveler name, pickup/destination, server-derived estimated Operator retained Fare, 15-second foreground response window, Accept Traveler, Decline, and normal missed-request re-offer. **Real defect identified:** the Decline button called the *timeout* `closeRequest()` path, whose implementation only calls `op.lapseRequest()` and leaves the Travel assigned; this is not an intentional rejection and can delay matching despite a deliberate user tap. Candidate preserves ordinary countdown lapse/re-offer but routes explicit Decline to already-existing authoritative `/travel/progress` refusal through `op.declineRequest()`. Do not close the visible sheet until backend confirms; errors keep the offer and display retry; guard simultaneous Accept/Decline and timeout. The server and payment rules are unchanged, no new API/reads/fees, no driver or real Traveler synthetic actions. Twice-run source regression asserts server acknowledgment, duplicate guards, and separate timeout behavior. **Additional verified UX defects:** the 15-second numeral restarted on phone resume but its animated progress bar stayed stopped; restoring both avoids contradictory visual cues. The fallback English-only `Pickup` / `Destination` labels now reuse existing five-language keys, and small low-contrast route and revenue labels use readable existing color/size tokens. No fabricated pickup ETA, hidden live fee, external request or new screen was added. Test on installed iOS and Android with delayed/failing networking and confirm live provider behavior before deployment. Research: [Apple feedback](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/feedback), [NN/g visibility of system status](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/visibility-system-status/), [Amazon idempotent retries](https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/making-retries-safe-with-idempotent-APIs/), [NASA intended-environment validation](https://swehb.nasa.gov/spaces/SWEHBVD/pages/102695440/SWE-055%2B-%2BRequirements%2BValidation). Council is a reference rubric, not a literal consultation.

**2026-10-17 SCHEDULED TRAVEL MULTIPLE BOOKINGS & CANCELLATION AUTHORITY — UNMERGED PR #52:** Complete service audit found a concrete cross-layer defect: Firestore rules allowed an authenticated Traveler to directly delete their scheduled reservation, even during or after dispatch, evading the server's atomic reserved-only cancellation and destroying evidence. Draft candidate now denies all direct scheduled-rides client writes and adversarial emulator tests assert both reserved/dispatched deletions and forged cancellation are refused. The existing server's protected transaction remains the sole cancellation authority. The client also fetched all years of scheduled records and displayed only one future Travel. Candidate adds bounded per-Traveler future-index queries, 20-booking paginated Upcoming Travel with individual status and deliberate per-record cancellation, links from Home and Menu, and a saved-payment-method entry from the scheduling review; no paid provider or changed fare. Google Firestore documentation supports ordered cursor pagination, but **the new `scheduled_rides(travelerUid,atMs)` composite index has NOT been verified READY in live Firebase**. This is a new required release gate, plus exact-SHA six workflows, real device and sandbox end-to-end tests. An already confirmed booking cannot yet be edited atomically; do not claim an Edit booking feature or a refund after assignment. [Research and test audit](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md). Do not merge or deploy merely because source/emulator tests pass.

**2026-10-17 COMPLETE SCHEDULED-TRAVEL SERVICE REVIEW — DRAFT PR #52:** Founder corrected a screen-centric, arbitrary-resizing review: design must start from the full customer experience, existing shared visual rubric, vehicle choice and post-booking management. Independently read current Uber Reserve, Lyft Scheduled Rides, Blacklane workflow, Apple HIG, Nielsen Norman Group consistency, GOV.UK review and Android accessibility, and cross-checked existing American Rider code. The scheduling summary now exposes actual bookable class and party/person, with Change rows linking to the existing Travel Confirmation selector and one authoritative server quote (no extra vendor calls). Restored the shared 44-unit visual sizing rather than making this screen inconsistent; safe Android touch widths still require physical device measurements. Home now requires explicit Keep/Cancel decision before server-confirmed cancellation of only a reserved/unassigned Travel, and the Scheduled travel menu stops routing to a new booking form. Cancellation after dispatch belongs to the actual active Travel lifecycle. Existing cancellation can be free while genuinely reserved; do not import competitor fees. **Outstanding product capability, not silently claimed:** editing an already accepted reservation is not implemented as a server-authoritative priced transactional operation. Do not add a decorative Edit button, unpriced client-side mutation or duplicate bookings. Tests and [whole-service audit](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md) added. Require six exact-head CI gates and signed iPhone/Android UX, cancellation-race and sandbox-payment proof before merging or publishing; this candidate does not change geography, fares, money or real-world approvals. **Additional verified architectural gap:** `src/backend/scheduled.ts` currently reads the Traveler's entire scheduled history without a limit, then displays only the earliest active reservation. This can increase Firestore reads over account lifetime and hides the remaining future reservations from management. A safe upcoming-reservations list requires an indexed, bounded, pageable query plus a status-aware management screen, new authentic index commissioning and regression; do not claim this is already implemented.

**2026-10-17 INSTITUTION-FIRST PLAN AHEAD FOLLOW-UP — UNMERGED REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Reviewed published MIT Sloan digital customer co-design, Harvard Business School service reliability, Stanford d.school/IDEO design validation, Apple picker and accessibility guidance, Nielsen Norman Group progressive disclosure, Baymard mobile checkout clarity, Google Firestore efficiency, Amazon retry identity, and NASA requirements validation against current Plan Ahead and the Council standard. Confirmed one editable itinerary, absolute pickup-zone calendar, one authoritative Total, existing quote/search reuse, no new recurring reads, and server-side scheduling identity. Found and corrected two further user-trust defects: a booked confirmation described as relative “Tomorrow” despite midnight rollover; and AM/PM selection silently replacing a previously chosen 6:00 with 6:30. The saved appointment now carries a visible exact weekday/date/year, and changing AM/PM preserves its hour. A further recovery fix preserves a previously chosen custom day and calendar month after returning from destination search. The final Schedule Travel review now also repeats Pickup location, Destination, exact scheduled day/time and the server Total together, correcting the former date-only “Pickup” summary and preventing missing context on narrow phones. Twice-run regression updated; [research and acceptance matrix](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md). This records researched recommendations, not interviews or endorsements. Pending six checks on exact final SHA plus real physical iPhone/Android visual, enlarged-text, screen-reader, narrow calendar-touch and signed booking evidence. **Do not merge PR #52 or deploy based on simulated tests alone.** No pricing, paid money, geography, insurance, screening or operator qualification changed.

**2026-10-17 SCHEDULED PICKUP LOOKUP STATE — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Further whole-screen audit caught another misleading state: while the app was still checking whether the selected pickup area could schedule Travel, it could temporarily display an unavailable warning. The screen now shows a neutral **Checking pickup area…** message while that existing market lookup is pending, and reserves the actual unavailable wording for completed failures. Five translations and twice-run source tests added, with **no extra network requests, Firestore reads, prices or vendors**. This addresses the user journey, not merely the original screenshot. A confirmed failed pickup-area lookup now offers **Try again**, which repeats the existing public market check only on the Traveler's tap, rather than polling or adding routine reads. This avoids a dead-end after transient connection errors. Physical-device testing is still required.

**2026-10-17 FULL PLAN AHEAD ITINERARY AND HUMAN-CENTERED UX — PR #52 REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Founder requested a whole-screen redesign, not merely the false time warning. **Source-verified Home entry legitimately starts with a chosen time before destination; that choice is preserved.** Published [Apple design/layout/picker/accessibility](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/design-principles/), [NN/g user journeys](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/user-journeys-vs-user-flows/), [Baymard mobile checkout](https://baymard.com/research-articles/current-state-of-checkout-ux), [GOV.UK dates](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/dates/), [IDEO journey mapping](https://www.designkit.org/methods/journey-map.html), [Stanford d.school](https://dschool.stanford.edu/tools/design-project-guide), [HBS service excellence](https://www.hbs.edu/faculty/Pages/profile.aspx?facId=6587), Google document-read costing, Amazon idempotent retries and NASA SWE-055 formed the decision record **before** UI changes, then were cross-checked against the ledger and actual code. The Plan ahead screen now visibly includes **two distinct editable rows, Pickup and Destination, at the top in one itinerary card**. Each uses existing Travel Confirmation search, retaining the chosen time and returning without immediate paid Travel. Today/Tomorrow/Choose date remain, with explicit calendar date for midnight clarity. A compact selected-time control reveals AM/PM, quick choices and custom quarter-hour entry only on demand. Complete Travel Cost is **Not quoted yet** until the current route and class receive the authoritative server quote; one primary button reads **Choose destination**, **Review Travel Cost**, or **Schedule Travel**, by state. Friendly pickup-local time is shown instead of IANA technical naming. Five languages and two-pass synthetic regressions cover hierarchy, fields, navigation, 44-point-high touch controls, screen reader labels, server quote, route pin, DST, clock, midnight, no extra reads and no accidental immediate confirmation. Full decisions and provenance: [Scheduled Travel UX review](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md). **No new SDK, vendor, billing/price engine, fee, live reservation, TestFlight or production deploy.** The PR must pass exact-head CI and real iPhone/Android usability/accessibility checks before merge; source and synthetic tests are not user-device acceptance. Council is used as a published-reference rubric, not actual personal endorsements.

**2026-10-17 SCHEDULED ITINERARY INTERACTION CHECK — PR #52:** The improved one-card pickup/destination itinerary uses the existing Travel Confirmation editors and a compact optional time selector. A follow-up inspection caught an unhandled `pickup=1` parameter: tapping **Edit pickup** would previously open Travel Confirmation without activating its pickup search. The destination and pickup editing paths now both activate the correct existing fields and preserve the scheduled-Travel return; a two-pass regression asserts link, route parameter and editor activation. No new search vendor, data collection, Stripe action or booking. Release/device acceptance still pending.

**2026-10-16 SCHEDULED TRAVEL FALSE TIME/PAYMENT VALIDATION — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Founder-supplied iPhone screen shows **Tomorrow · 6:00 AM**, home pickup **18631 SW 97th Pl**, a blank destination, **$0.00** and the error **“It is 11:36 PM, so these times are no longer available. Choose PM or another day.”** Source inspection established no evidence that tomorrow's 6 AM was actually rejected as past. `app/schedule.tsx` combined genuine past-time, absent server Travel Total, missing pickup zone and incomplete date into one `schedInvalid` Boolean; rendered the same expired-time notice for all; and printed `fmt(ride.travelerTotal)` even though zero is its “no current server quote” sentinel. The Home “Schedule Travel” button intentionally permits selecting a day **before** choosing a destination (founder-settled 2026-09-13), so missing destination at that moment is an expected, recoverable state, **not an error**. Research-first correction follows [NN/g inline forms and error-message guidance](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/errors-forms-design-guidelines/), [Apple HIG restrained actionable alerts](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/alerts), [Expo 57 Router navigation](https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/sdk/router/), [Google Firestore billable reads](https://cloud.google.com/firestore/pricing), [Amazon idempotent retries](https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/making-retries-safe-with-idempotent-APIs/), and [NASA SWE-055 intended-environment validation](https://swehb.nasa.gov/spaces/SWEHBVD/pages/102695440/SWE-055%2B-%2BRequirements%2BValidation). The proposed client `scheduleReadiness` separates missing destination, absent **authoritative, class-matched** Total, pickup zone/date, unconfirmed custom times, DST invalidity and real expired instants. On screenshot state it shows a destination-selection action and a dash rather than a fabricated $0 quote or time error; choosing a destination uses the **existing** Travel Confirmation search/quote pathway. The destination search now carries a lightweight `scheduling=1` **screen-navigation hint** (not a booking or payment instruction), so Travel Confirmation says **Scheduled pickup / Continue** instead of the incorrect **Leaving now / Change** while returning to the already selected day and time. No additional API requests are created. **Further end-to-end review repaired two linked failures:** destination search returning to Travel Confirmation now presents a clear **Continue to Schedule** action, not the immediate-money **Confirm Travel** button (five languages); and scheduled reservations now transmit the current map-selected pickup pin instead of stale old route coordinates. Current route geometry and completed pickup repricing are required before treating a quote as ready. The submit button rechecks the actual clock at tap, and the displayed time-zone abbreviation is calculated for the **selected pickup instant** (including DST) rather than today's abbreviation. The market time-zone lookup now uses the current selected pickup pin (or the quoted route pickup), not a stale named departure; this preserves national time-zone correctness after pin movement and uses the existing cached market lookup. These changes do not revise amounts or create new network queries. Existing midnight-aware market-local date resolution and server schedule/price authority remain intact; a clock-only 30-second foreground update and phone-resume update introduce **zero new network calls or Firestore reads**. The previous blanket warning is replaced by genuine time validation across five languages; typing an invalid or merely suggested time no longer silently schedules the old selected slot. Two-pass source and pure-state tests cover screenshot at 23:36 EDT, tomorrow 06:00, boundary instants, missing inputs and DST overlap/gap. ****Midnight invariant:** a chosen Today/Tomorrow/explicit calendar date now has one absolute market-local YYYY-MM-DD in the shared Traveler context. During foreground clock refresh, the selected day never shifts silently; the Today/Tomorrow/Pick day chip and summary adapt to that SAME saved date. It persists while the Traveler chooses a destination and returns, and clears for a genuinely new journey. Time and DST validation still use the saved market-local instant. Two-pass regression covers 23:36→00:01 midnight crossing and route-return continuity. No fare change, booking, Stripe charge, Travel creation, market status, provider subscription or live deployment.** Await exact-head CI and installed-device tests before merge/release.

**2026-10-17 ACCOUNT EMAIL WHOLE-JOURNEY CONSOLIDATION — DRAFT PR #53:** The design review found a second, outdated email-change form inside Sign-in and security that still displayed Continue and returned to idle after merely sending a link. That form is removed; Account Details and Sign-in and security now reach the same `app/account-email.tsx`, including pending/verified state, authenticated reset, real correction, and provider-specific support. The pending address is stored only in UID-scoped local storage, wiped on account switch/sign-out, with no password/token persistence; the visual design uses standard Card/PrimaryButton dimensions. Verified Firebase address changes also force a fresh identity token exactly once, so subsequent Travel and receipt requests see the new authenticated email instead of a stale ID token. Published Apple, OWASP and Firebase guidance checked. Added twice-run source contracts and kept the existing password-change route. This draft has NOT sent any email or changed a real account. The legacy separate Firestore profile `email` field is not yet certified as synchronized; Firebase auth token remains server operational authority. Require exact SHA six workflow passes, real device sign-in/link/restart/offline/wrong-password checks, and Firebase existing-email notification verification before release. [Decision ADR 0029](docs/decisions/0029-verified-email-change-user-journey.md).

Read Apple input/label HIG, OWASP sensitive identity changes, NIST account notification, Stanford user-testing guidance, council standard and real `app/account-email.tsx` / Firebase Auth. Kept shared screen, cards and primary button sizes; improved secondary-text contrast using existing ink2 rather than arbitrarily enlarging typography. No new Firestore reads, recurring checks, email vendor or invented success. All five locale copy updated and new twice-run source regression included. An account-scoped local reminder now retains the pending proposed address across app restarts (not the password or link token) and is cleared on confirmed change, correction, and account sign-out. **Unverified:** Firebase existing-address change notification, live email delivery and handoff, actual forced-restart UI behavior, device autofill/keyboard/VoiceOver, and provider-specific reauthentication; no TestFlight or deployment. [ADR 0029](docs/decisions/0029-verified-email-change-user-journey.md). Exact PR-head CI and physical tests are required before merge.

No Render Stripe secret/account matching was proven and no payment was initiated. `src/backend/connect.ts` now distinguishes genuine server-confirmed unpaid Travel recovery from HTTP/transport/invalid-response uncertainty. Operator duty state tracks the latest account-bound payout check; local earnings and the Operator Revenue screen are explicitly described as device-saved estimates, never as verified Stripe transfers or bank deposits. Cached estimated earnings are now scoped by authenticated Operator UID, while the old shared-device key is left untouched but not displayed to another account. Operator payout education no longer promises immediate/automatic deposits and is localized in five languages.
